# Engine C2a: counting and repeats — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

> **Drafted overnight 2026-10-11 to 10-12 for the PI's review; revised 2026-10-12 to 10-13** after its engineering and science reviews, the PI's seven answers of 2026-10-12 and his answers of 2026-10-13 to Appendix B, which it builds as given (below, "The PI's decisions it builds"). It re-asks none of his decisions. **Appendix B is answered**: he approved its reading rules (items 1 and 2), chose that each task names where its stimulus appears (item 3, "Task names it, required"), and accepted the reference tasks' tables with leaving the target early a target break in both detection tasks and no limit in `visual_search_training` (items 5 to 13, "Accept, target break"). **No welfare-critical function changes** (Appendix A, three items for his summary, all informational, still to be asked with the plan's approval).
>
> **Build C2 splits in two** (`c2-scope.md`, 2026-10-11, §3): **C2a, counting and repeats** (this plan: no welfare-listed code, no plan vocabulary, testable through `RunSpec.blocks` and the scheduler, the four defects fixed before plans make them reachable) and **C2b, plans and blocks** (plans in the task object, choosing a plan at run start, block-sequence policies, block and run time limits, the progress check, plan tables in the review report). "Not in C2a" lists C2b's part.
>
> **Base.** Every code reference was read at `engine-c1` `2d8717b` (engine build C1, built and reviewed, merging into `main` once its CI reads clean) and again on the **warnings build's verified tip** (`scratchpad/wor2`, `f070c4c`, XC-352's changes, which the PI put before C2a on 2026-10-11: a live or starting value outside its range re-checked and accepted with a warning unless it would break the run; several load-time findings made warnings). **C2a's branch is cut from `main` after C1 and the warnings build merge, and this plan is written against the warnings build's tip**: Task 5 rewrites its `check.TRIAL_CAP` and the test that pins it, and Task 10 one of its tests' expected findings, so neither applies at `2d8717b` alone. The controller re-reads each anchor on the branch as cut before Task 1 and moves any that changed, never the code. **If the warnings build raised `link.SCHEMA`**, Task 9 takes the next number, and its schema pins, history line and test names follow it. **C2a's own new checks keep to the warnings-over-refusals rule**: its refusals are authoring rules the PI named (a structure without a counting table, or whose table names no state its stimulus appears in, or one it does not have), and one warning (`hold-cut-short`). **Backlog IDs**: this plan's lines take **XC-361 to XC-365 and XC-367**, within XC-360 to XC-369, which are C2a's (XC-370 to XC-373 are the warnings build's, whose XC-373 is the once-a-second checks inside a long trial); "Next free" stays the warnings build's. **Decisions**: **TSK-52 to TSK-56** (the warnings build, which lands first, takes TSK-51); no code or test names a decision's number.

> **Revised 2026-10-18: how trials count** (the PI's sixteen decisions of that day, below). With Tasks 1 to 6 built and closed, Task 7 committed with its review asking for fixes and Task 8 committed and unreviewed, the PI stopped the build: "I am confused what is going on here. Where did we decide a correct answer resets a counter? ... I think it may be useful for us to define the ways in which trials count and at what levels, e.g., whole-run, in-block, required streaks, drumming, as well as the statistics for run reporting and monitoring." A map of how trials count went to him, and he answered sixteen questions; then: **"Revise the plan, show me, then build"**. **What changed**, and why -- each his decision, built:
> - **Judging** (decisions 1 to 4, 14): a rule judges every answer and only answers, whatever the table says about paying; under drumming a streak judges every try in order, and a percentage too unless a trainer sets it to first answers only. Task 12.
> - **The repeat limit and *clear*** (decisions 7 to 9): any answer ends a trial's failed tries; the task's limit holds in every block unless the block sets one; *clear one* starts the count over. Task 7's fix round and Task 8's.
> - **Criterion blocks** (decision 6): a target is a minimum; past them every condition is drawn until the criterion is met (XC-364 settled). Task 13.
> - **Training aids** (decisions 10 to 13): drumming and *clear* are training aids, *clear* off until switched on; a switch for it and the percentage setting, recorded with who; every row marks the aids; the console's standing warning, worded for the session's kind (Task 9). Task 14; per-animal profiles filed as XC-379 (Task 11).
> - **Reporting** (decisions 5, 15, 16): a trial and its drummed repeats are one trial; each condition's counts on the console and the run's end row; the strip's two percentages. Task 15, and Task 9 for the console.
> - **Unbuilt tasks revised in place**: Task 9 (the console), Task 11 (the record and docs); Task 10 is unchanged but for its counts, restated on this revision's base.
> - **Welfare**: one new item for the PI's approval (Appendix A, item 4: the training switch is a third branch in `Session._command`, beside the two he approved, outside its listed parts); no welfare limit changes. **Appendix B** gains items 14 to 22, the engineering calls this revision made that he may want to see.

**Goal:** *(Revised 2026-10-18: and every answer is judged for a block's rule, a criterion block's targets are a minimum, drumming and* clear *are training aids, and a trial and its drummed repeats are one trial, each condition's counts on the console and the run's end row.)* Each trial structure declares what every outcome does to its condition's count -- counted, used up, shown again, or (under "until N correct") paying nothing -- and the scheduler counts by it, puts a repeat at a free random slot within the next few trials and never the very next, drums a trial back until it is right when asked, forgives a condition's debt past its repeat limit or when a person clears one trial of it, or the rest, from the console, ends a trial at five minutes as a missed acquisition, and records all of it in each trial's row, each run's end row and the console; and the reference tasks let a hold run its own time.

**Architecture:** `wl_xcon/task.py` gains the counting table (`Counting`, with the classes `COUNTED`, `SPENT`, `OWED`, `UNPAID`, the right answers `RIGHT` and the fixed groups `ANSWERS`, `REPEATED`, `AFTER_STIMULUS`) and `Trial.counting`; `check.check_task` refuses a structure without one, `check` warns where a timeout can cut a hold short, and the review report shows each table. `wl_xcon/scheduler.py` replaces its outcome sets with the table (`Scheduler.counting`, a block's own in `Block.counting`), and gains repeat placement (`Draw`, `_repeat`, `next_trial`), drumming (`Block.drum`, `Scheduler.drum`, `drum_tries`), the repeat limit and the console's clear (`Shortfall`, `_forgive`, `clear`, `clearable`). `wl_xcon/run.py` derives the longest trial from 300 s at the session's rate. `taskd` passes the default structure's table, writes each trial's draw and count into its row and the shortfalls into the run's end row, puts the hang's and the drum's warnings on the changes feed, and routes two new console commands (`link.ClearOwed`, `link.Drum`) beside the pause; telemetry schema 16 carries the shortfalls, drumming and whether anything can be cleared, and the page's Still-needed pane offers *clear one*, *clear the rest* and the drumming switches. The reference tasks bound their holds by backstops longer than any legal hold, and `adaptive_detection`'s staircase steps up on a miss.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.11-3.13; dataclasses; pytest; Playwright for the page's one new browser test (as `tests/test_page_browser.py` already runs). **No new dependency.**

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md` (approved 2026-10-07) §16 (all items; 4 and 5 in part, see "Not in C2a"; item 6 as the PI read it on 2026-10-12), §6.8 (the longest trial), §13.5 only for the per-block declaration of what counts; `docs/references/decisions.md` TSK-26 to TSK-35; backlog XC-287 (closed by Task 5 and Task 11). The scope is build C2's research map (`.../scratchpad/c2-scope.md`, 2026-10-11): its §1 items 7 to 11 and 14, §2's four defects, §3's C2a list, §4's refusals and §6's risks, every file:line citation re-read at `2d8717b`. The reviews: `.../scratchpad/c2a-plan-review-eng.md` and `c2a-plan-review-sci.md` (2026-10-12).

## The PI's decisions it builds (binding)

**Of 2026-10-11, for build C2:**

1. **"A repeated trial in a block with no fixed length (criterion- or time-ended, or drawing with replacement) goes at a random slot within the next k trials, k = the block's number of conditions, at least 2, never the very next."** Extended to every block and k at least 3 by his answers 3 and 4 below. Built: `Scheduler._repeat` and `next_trial` (Task 4); decisions.md TSK-30, amended.
2. **Drumming, "a training tool ... where a trial is repeated until correct"**: its own option; the same trial comes back **immediately** until correct; switched on per block or run by the trainer, and recorded as a change. Built: `Block.drum`, `Scheduler.drum` and `drumming` (Task 6), the console's `Drum` command (Task 8) and its switches on the page (Task 9). **Recorded in C2a** as a `controls.jsonl` row naming who switched it, from what to what, and in every trial's row (`drumming`, and `drummed` for a drum's repeat); **build C3's change log takes it over with its reason** (XC-363). decisions.md TSK-52.
3. **A trial hitting the trial cap is repeated like a fault, but it is a missed acquisition, not a fixation break** ("it isn't a fixation break, it is a missed acquisition"); the cap is §6.8's, derived from 300 s at the session's rate. Built: `task.HANG_COUNTS_AS` (Task 1), counted by the scheduler (Task 3), shown again (Task 4), `run.LONGEST_TRIAL_S` and `longest_trial_frames` (Task 5); XC-287 closed; decisions.md TSK-55.
4. **A break after the stimulus appears: no default; each task declares whether it is used up or shown again; the load-time check refuses a task that does not say** (an authoring rule). Built: `Counting.after_stimulus`, required (Task 1), `check_task`'s `undeclared-counting` (Task 2), each reference task's answer (Task 2; Appendix B); decisions.md TSK-29, amended. Where the stimulus appears is his answer of 2026-10-13 below.
5. **Repeat cap when a task sets none: no limit (repeat until done), and a console control to manually advance past, or clear a specific trial from, the count** *(refined 2026-10-18, decisions 9 and 10 below: clearing one trial starts its count over, and "clear" is a training aid, off until a trainer switches it on)* ("there should be some way in the console to manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count"), recorded. Built: `Counting.cap`, `None` by default (Task 1), the limit's forgiving (Task 7), `Scheduler.clear`, `link.ClearOwed` and `Session._clear` (Task 8), *clear one* on the page (Task 9). **Routed outside `Session._command`'s welfare-listed parts**: two branches beside the pause, its `held` pass-through and its one `except` line unchanged, compared in Task 11 (Appendix A, item 1). decisions.md TSK-54.
6. **"No response" in both detection tasks is a miss (counts, used up).** Built as an answer, counted -- never marked an abort, which is what this plan's "used up" means (the science review's O4): `Counting(withholding=True)` in `fixation_detection` and `adaptive_detection` (Task 2; Appendix B, items 5 and 7); decisions.md TSK-53.
7. **A "balanced" block order balances within each run.** Recorded here for build C2b, which builds the block-sequence policies (decisions.md TSK-56); nothing in C2a uses it.
8. **The PI's standing rule (2026-10-11): warnings over refusals** -- "warnings are more appropriate most of the time with an explanation of why it may not be a good idea ... the experimenter needs free control most of the time". C2a refuses only where an authoring rule or an uninterpretable record demands it, and says which: a structure without a counting table, or whose table names no state its stimulus appears in or one it does not have (authoring rules, decision 4 and his answer of 2026-10-13), and a malformed table or command, refused as it is built or decoded (unreadable). A console *clear* of a condition that owes nothing changes nothing and is refused with its sentence, as a pause of a paused session is. Its one new load-time check, a timeout that can cut a hold short, is a warning; a hang and a long drum are warnings on the console.

**Of 2026-10-12, answering the plan's first draft (each the option he chose, quoted):**

1. **Drummed repeats and the criterion: "Leave drummed repeats out"** -- "Only each trial's first try is judged for the criterion. The repeats are still recorded as what they were." Built: `Scheduler.record` judges only a first try (Task 6); TSK-52. ***Refined 2026-10-18*** *(decisions 3 and 4 below): now the training setting "first answers only", for a percentage rule; a streak, and a percentage by default, judge every answer.*
2. **The drum's warning: "After 10 in a row"** -- "A warning on the console after 10 failed tries in a row" of one drummed trial; drumming goes on. Built: `scheduler.DRUM_WARNING`, `Scheduler.drum_tries` and the changes feed's warning (Task 6); TSK-52.
3. **Fixed-length blocks: "Same rule, within the next k"** -- "One rule for every block. Repeats don't pile up at the block's end." Built: `_repeat`, for every order and end rule (Task 4); TSK-30.
4. **The smallest k: "At least 3"** -- "A repeat lands 2 or 3 trials later even in a two-condition block, so its position is not fixed." k = max(3, the block's number of conditions). Built: `_repeat` (Task 4); TSK-30.
5. **What a task's repeat limit counts: "Tries in a row of one trial"** -- "The condition is moved past after 3 failed repeats in a row, and the count resets once it is done." Built: `Counts.in_a_row`, reset when the condition pays (Task 7); §16.6 amended, quoting him (Task 11); TSK-31 and TSK-54. ***Refined 2026-10-18*** *(decision 7 below): the count resets at any answer, paid or not -- "1: an answer ends the trial" (Task 7's fix round).*
6. **Calibration's limit: "No limit"** -- "As drafted. The trainer moves past a target with the console's 'clear' button." Built: `calibration`'s table sets none (Task 2; Appendix B item 11); TSK-53. ***Refined 2026-10-18*** *(decision 12 below): "clear" is a training aid, off by default; the trainer switches it on to move past a target.*
7. **The staircase: "Yes, a miss steps contrast up"** -- "A miss counts as an incorrect answer, so after misses the target gets more visible." Built: `adaptive_detection.next_params` (Task 10); TSK-34, revised.

**Of 2026-10-13, answering Appendix B (each the option he chose, quoted):**

1. **Items 1 and 2, the reading rules: "Approve".**
2. **Item 3, where a stimulus appears: "Task names it, required"** -- "Each task's counting table names the step where its stimulus appears, and the load check refuses a table without one. A cued task names the cue step. A small extra declaration in every task; the five test tasks name their target or array step." Built: `Counting.appears` (Task 1); `check_task` refusing a structure whose table names none (`undeclared-counting`) or a state it does not have (`bad-counting`), and each reference task's (Task 2); `taskd._appeared` reading it (Task 3); TSK-29, amended.
3. **Items 5 to 13, the reference tasks' tables: "Accept, target break"** -- "Take the plan's tables, make leaving the target early a target break in the two detection tasks, and give visual_search_training no repeat limit." Built: the tables as proposed (Task 2); `Exited("target")` during the hold a `TARGET_BREAK` in `fixation_detection` and `adaptive_detection` (Task 10); TSK-53. The question said these tables are engine test material, run only in the simulator, and that real tasks' tables are set with him in wl-xtasks.

**Of 2026-10-18, during the build** (asked on Task 6's review, minor 2: "With drumming on ... what if that first try ends before the animal answers ... and it then answers on the drummed repeat?"):

- **"Judge its first answer"** -- "If the first try ends before any answer, the first try that does answer is judged. Repeats after a wrong answer stay out, as you chose." Built: `Scheduler._answered` (Task 6's fix round, `c1d52e9`). ***Holds inside the training setting "first answers only"*** *(decision 4 below; Task 12).*

**Of 2026-10-18, how trials count** (his note of that day stopped the build; asked one question at a time, each the option he chose or his own words, quoted from the build's ledger; the map he answered from is `.superpowers/counting-map/counting-map.md` in the main checkout). These supersede or refine the answers above where marked:

1. **Answers only: "2: only answers make or break a streak"** (asked: '"5 correct in a row": right, then a fixation break (no answer), then right again. Is the streak now 2, or back to 1?'). A rule judges answers only -- right (correct, a correct rejection) or wrong (a wrong target, a false alarm, an early or a late response, a miss where the task makes no response an answer); a trial with no answer (a fixation break, no fixation, a lost tracker, a hang) neither adds to nor breaks a streak or a percentage. Built: `Scheduler._answer` (Task 12).
2. **Every answer is judged: "Yes, every answer counts in the 20"** (asked about XC-367: under "until N correct", is a wrong answer judged for the percentage?) -- "the percentage judges every answer, right and wrong, whatever the counting says about paying". Built: Task 12; XC-367 closed unfiled (Task 11).
3. **A streak under drumming: "Every answer counts in order"** -- "First tries and drummed tries alike: the wrong answer breaks the streak (back to 0), and the drummed correct starts it again at 1. A streak can only be built from correct answers in a row, whichever try they came on." Built: Task 12 (a streak is a rule of proportion 1.0; Appendix B item 14).
4. **A percentage under drumming: a training setting, default "Every answer"** -- his words: "Perhaps a toggle in training? Drumming should only be used in training sessions, so perhaps in the training section, there is a toggle for how they are counted." Asked its default: "Every answer" -- "drummed answers go into the percentage in order by default, as for a streak; 'first answers only' is the setting switched to on purpose". Built: `Scheduler.first_answers` (Task 12), switched from the console (Task 14) and on the page (Task 9).
5. **"200 trials; drummed repeats inside them"** (asked: 'In a training run set to "200 trials" with drumming on, what is 200?') -- "A trial and its drummed repeats are one of the 200 ... The console would show both: trials (200) and presentations (e.g. 260)." Every try keeps its own line and trial number in the record. Built: Task 15 (each condition's trials and presentations), Task 9 (the console).
6. **A criterion block's targets: "Both: criterion and every target"** -- "The block ends only once the criterion is met and every target is used up; conditions keep coming until both hold." Built: Task 13; XC-364 closed unfiled.
7. **What resets the repeat limit: "1: an answer ends the trial"** (asked: "Say condition A's trial breaks twice, then is answered wrong; later another trial of A breaks. Is that break failure 1 or failure 3?"). Supersedes answer 5 of 2026-10-12's reset "once the condition pays" (as built). Built: Task 7's fix round.
8. **Whose limit: "No: the task's limit applies unless the block sets its own"**. Built: Task 7's fix round.
9. **Clear one: "End that waiting trial; count starts over"**. Built: Task 8's fix round.
10. **The training aids: "Drumming and 'clear'"** -- "clearing trials from the count is a training aid too: off by default, with the standing warning while it is available"; and, of the aids, "By default training aids should be off, unless we have training default profiles. And there can be a warning that training aids are on in the console." Built: Task 14 (*clear* off until switched on; every row marks the aids), Task 9 (the warning).
11. **Outside a training session: "Allow it, with a warning"** -- "it works, and the console shows a standing warning that a training aid is on in a non-training session. The record says so on every trial." Built: Tasks 14 and 9.
12. **Calibration: "Trainer turns 'clear' on when needed"** -- no exception: if a target sticks, the trainer switches *clear* on, with the standing warning, and moves past it. Supersedes calibration's reliance on an always-available *clear* (answer 6 of 2026-10-12). Built: Task 14.
13. **Per-animal training profiles** -- "It is nice if the training sessions are included in the profiles for loading in the circumstances where you want to resume with the same settings as the previous day with a monkey." Read back as a separate, larger piece of work, beside the training records (XC-288), and confirmed ("Yes, that's it"). Filed: XC-379 (Task 11).
14. **Early and late responses: "Both wrong answers (as built)"** -- each an answer that is not right: it pays the trial, counts against a percentage, breaks a streak, and is drummed. Built as before; `task.Family`'s docstring says so (Task 12).
15. **The strip under drumming: "Show both"** -- "two percentages side by side: right first time (per trial, a trial and its drummed repeats one) and right per presentation (every try)". Built: Task 15 (each level's drummed tries), Task 9 (the strip).
16. **Per condition, all four** -- trials and presentations; right, wrong, % right (first time); owed, repeats, moved past (with the avoidance flag); drummed tries (and the current failed run); "all of them also written into the run's end record" (the question's proposal, which he did not object to). Built: Task 15 (the end row's `conditions`), Task 9 (the console).

And on how C2a goes on: **"Revise the plan, show me, then build"** -- this revision, and its summary for him.

**Of 2026-10-18, after the revision.** He approved it: the plan; Appendix A item 4, **"Approve"**; Appendix B items 14 to 19, 21 and 22, **"Approve all"**; and item 20's scheduled stop, **"Keep counting presentations"** -- the console labels it as counting presentations, drummed tries included. Then, asked two questions from its engineering review:

17. **A hold-training block: "Yes, where the block says so"** (asked: 'Take a block that trains a hold, e.g. "hold fixation; done at 80% held over the last 10". Under your rule that only answers count, a broken hold is left out, so the rule is met by any 10 holds, even if the animal broke 9 times in 10. Should a broken hold count as a miss in such a block?') -- "Only where a block itself declares a broken hold as a scored trial (a hold-training block) does it count as a miss toward the percentage. Everywhere else, breaks stay out, as you decided." Refines decision 1. Built: `Scheduler._scored` (Task 12).
18. **A drum at a percentage block's end: "Finish the drum first"** (asked: 'With drumming on, a block's percentage can be reached on a wrong answer, e.g. 4 right in a row then a wrong one still makes 80% of the last 5. Should the block end right there, or first bring that trial back until it is right?') -- "The block ends only after the drummed trial is answered right. If the extra tries drop the percentage below the bar, the block carries on. Training ends on a success." Built: `Scheduler.finished` (Task 13).
19. **A fixation-training block: "Yes, where the block says so"** (asked on Task 12's review: "should the same apply to a block that trains looking at the fixation point at all, so that a failure to acquire it (including a trial that times out waiting at five minutes) counts as a miss where that block says so?") -- "Same rule as broken holds ... Lost tracking and rig faults never count against the animal." Built: `Scheduler._scored` (Task 12's fix round).

## What the PI should know

Things this plan does or does not do, in plain terms, beyond Appendix B.

1. **Every task must say, for each of its trial structures, what a break after the stimulus appears does** -- used up or shown again -- or it will not load (your decision 4). The five reference tasks' answers are Appendix B, which you confirmed on 2026-10-13.
2. **Every task also names the step where its stimulus appears** (your answer of 2026-10-13, "Task names it, required"), or it will not load -- nor will one naming a step it does not have. A break before that step -- a long blink during the fixation hold -- ended the trial before anything was presented, so it is shown again like a fixation break, even in a task that uses breaks up. A cued task names its cue step, so a break in a memory-guided saccade's delay counts as after the stimulus. The five test tasks name their target's or array's step, and calibration its target's.
3. **Repeats follow your rule in every block** (your answers of 2026-10-12): a random slot within the next k trials, k the number of conditions and at least 3, never the very next. Each repeat takes a slot no other waiting repeat holds, so none comes later than its k (a measured 0 in tens of thousands). While a repeat waits, its condition is not drawn otherwise. When nothing else can be drawn -- it is the only condition still owed, or every other is waiting too -- the waiting repeat comes now, and its row says if it came straight after the trial it repeats.
4. **Drumming** is switched on by a block that declares it (build C2b's plans will) or from the console: *drum this block*, *drum this run*, or *stop drumming*. Every trial that is not right -- a wrong answer, a break, a hang; a correct rejection is right -- comes straight back and pays nothing until it is right. **Only each trial's first try is judged for a criterion** (your answer; *revised 2026-10-18: a streak judges every try in order, and a percentage too unless you set it to first answers only, your decisions 3 and 4*), and **after 10 failed tries in a row the console's changes feed says so**, naming the condition and the trial, while drumming goes on (your answer). A switch made just as a block ends is for the next block. **It cannot be switched on as a run starts from the page**: a run's start goes through `Service._start`, which is welfare-listed, so you switch it on right after starting. Recorded as a control row and in every trial's row; your change log (build C3) takes it over with its reason.
5. **The five-minute cap**: a trial reaching it is recorded `hang`, as before, and counted as a missed acquisition (your decision 3), so it is shown again; in a plain "run N trials" run it is one of the N, where until now a run ran N trials plus its hangs. **The console's changes feed says so**, naming the trial and what became of it, a warning with nothing stopped. The cap was 100,000 frames, about 417 s at 240 Hz; it is now 300 s at the session's rate (72,000 frames at 240 Hz), and the warnings build's warning about a wait longer than any the task declares, which names the cap that ends such a trial ("about 417 s today, 5 minutes once C2a is built", as you were asked it), now says five minutes.
6. **No repeat limit unless a task sets one** (your decision 5); where one is set it counts failed repeats in a row of one trial, starting again once the condition pays (your answer; *revised 2026-10-18: at any answer, paid or not, and the task's limit holds in every block unless the block sets its own, your decisions 7 and 8*). The page's *clear one*, beside each condition still owed (*since 2026-10-18 only while the training aid* clear *is switched on, and it starts the condition's count over, your decisions 9 and 10*), takes one trial off that condition's count, and *clear the rest*, beside a condition owing more than one, takes all it still owes (the science review: a refused condition owing 20 took 20 clicks); the run's end row records who cleared it. **Neither is offered where nothing can be owed** -- a plain "run N trials" run, unless its trials are drummed -- since a clear there would only shorten the run. **I read "clear a specific trial in the count" as moving past a trial still owed.** If you meant removing a trial that already counted (so the condition owes it again), that is another button of the same shape, not built.
7. **Under "run until ten correct", a wrong answer is not shown again**: it pays nothing and the block's order brings its condition back as it would anyway -- your rule of 2026-08-31, "wrong choice not" re-queued. The first draft had turned it into a correction trial; that is undone.
8. **Only the animal's own outcomes are flagged as possible avoidance.** A repeat limit reached with a lost tracker or a fault among the failed tries is named for the rig on the console and in the record, and not called the animal's.
9. **In both detection tasks, leaving the target before its hold is complete is now a target break** (your answer of 2026-10-13, "Accept, target break"), as `visual_search` already called it: shown again by their tables, strobed as a target break, and -- being no answer -- it no longer moves `adaptive_detection`'s staircase. It was a wrong answer, a distractor-type response in tasks that show no distractor.
10. **A held target was being scored a miss, and this plan fixes it.** In all four detection and search tasks the response deadline ran from the moment the eye landed on the target as well as from the target's onset, so setting `target_hold` longer than `response_deadline` -- the ordinary way to shape a longer hold -- ended every held target as no response: a miss, unrewarded. The deadline now bounds only the response; the hold runs its own time. **The same was true of the fixation hold** in every task, and of calibration's hold, against the acquisition time; they are fixed the same way. At the tasks' starting values nothing changes. A new load-time warning says when any task's timeout can cut a hold short.
11. **`adaptive_detection`'s staircase now steps contrast up after a miss** (your answer), and a broken target hold, now a target break, no longer steps it at all. Nothing calls the staircase during a run yet (XC-242), so this changes nothing live until it is.
12. **Until build C2b, every live run is the plain "run N trials" run** (the science review's O5), whose every-trial table replaces each task's: so Appendix B's tables, items 5 to 12, change nothing an animal experiences in C2a. What does: drumming, *clear*, the five-minute cap, the hold fix, and the detection tasks' target break, which the record and the console now name as one.
13. **Four defects are fixed, none of which reached a rig** (only tests gave a run blocks; build C2b's plans make them reachable): correct rejections and false alarms did not count, so a block of catch trials never ended; target and blink breaks were shown again whatever a task wanted; a repeat came back as the very next trial 8 times in 8 when drawing with replacement; and a hang neither counted nor came back.
14. **A failure found on the way, not fixed here**: a block that ends on a criterion, whose conditions all meet their targets before the criterion is met, has nothing left to draw and faults the run (it did before C2a too). Build C2b's per-advance progress check ends such a run with a sentence (XC-364). ***Fixed 2026-10-18*** *(your decision 6, Task 13): a criterion block's targets are a minimum, and past them every condition is drawn until the criterion is met; XC-364 is not filed.*
15. **Nothing on the welfare list changes** (Appendix A). The two console controls pass through `Session._command`, part of which is listed, as two new branches beside the pause; the listed parts are unchanged, byte for byte. *Since 2026-10-18 a third, the training switch (Task 14), beside them: Appendix A, item 4, for your approval.*
16. **Every reference task's content hash changes**: each now declares its counting table, which is part of what a task declares, and the four with a target hold, and calibration, bound their holds anew; the two detection tasks end a broken target hold as a target break.
17. **The training aids** (your decisions 10 to 12, Tasks 14 and 9): *clear* is off as every run starts until a trainer switches it on; drumming is off until a block declares it or a trainer switches it on, as before. While either is on the console shows a standing banner, in a piloting or recording session a warning that training aids are meant for training sessions, and every trial's row lists the aids on (`aids`). A *clear* sent while it is off is refused with a sentence and changes nothing. Each switch holds for its run (Appendix B, item 18).
18. **What the console shows now** (your decisions 15 and 16, Tasks 15 and 9; amended 2026-10-18 to show trials, a trial and its drummed repeats one, with the presentations beside them on the Working? pane and /health): the strip leads with right first time wherever trials were drummed, with right per presentation beside it; beneath each condition, its trials, presentations, right and wrong answers, percent right first time, repeats, trials moved past (and the flag), and drummed tries; and the run's end row records all of them, block by block.
19. **Two answers of 2026-10-18 after the revision** (decisions 17 and 18, Tasks 12 and 13): in a block whose own table counts a broken hold as paying -- a hold-training block -- the break counts as a miss toward its percentage, and nowhere else; and a block whose rule is met on a drummed wrong answer waits for the drum to be answered right, carrying on if the extra tries drop it below the bar.

## Calls taken in this plan

Engineering decisions made without asking; each says what it costs if wrong.

1. **The counting table is a declaration of each trial structure, `Trial.counting`** (engine spec §16.1: "per trial structure"), a `task.Counting` beside the rest of the task's vocabulary, so build C2b's conditions, which name their structure, count by it. `None` is undeclared, which `check_task` refuses in a task's structure; a bare `Trial` -- `check`'s, a test's -- needs none. In the content hash, so every reference task's hash moves. Cost: if the PI wants it on the task object, a dict keyed by structure; one field moves.
2. **Four classes: `counted`, `spent`, `owed` and `unpaid`** (§16.2's words: spent is "counted as presented and marked an abort", owed is "shown again later"; unpaid pays nothing and is not shown again). The fourth restores the PI's 2026-08-31 rule under "until N correct" (the controller's ruling on the engineering review's I3: "Not repeated: wrong targets"), so a wrong answer there is not a correction trial and never feeds the repeat limit. A block's own table replaces its structure's (`Block.counting`, TSK-26), and the old named sets become tables: `CORRECT_ONLY` (every answer but a right one unpaid), `PRESENTED` (`after_stimulus=SPENT`), `EVERY_TRIAL` (everything but an answer spent: the flat run's). An unpaid answer is not judged for a criterion unless the block's `criterion_over` names it, as `CORRECT_ONLY` judged only correct trials before -- so such a block's criterion is met by any N right answers, a trap filed for C2b, whose plans give blocks criteria (XC-367). *Revised 2026-10-18 (the PI's decision 2, Task 12): every answer is judged, unpaid ones too; `criterion_over` is removed; XC-367 is not filed.* `scheduler.Counting`, `COMPLETED`, `REQUEUED`, `Block.counts_toward` and `Block.requeue_on` are removed; nothing outside the tests used them. Cost: if the PI wants a wrong answer under "until ten correct" shown again, one line of `CORRECT_ONLY`.
3. **A repeat takes a free slot.** The PI's rule (his decision 1 and answers 3 and 4) picks a random slot within the next k trials; this plan picks it among the slots no other waiting repeat holds, so two never fall due together and none waits past its k (the engineering review's Minor 2). Every waiting repeat was placed at an earlier trial m with its slot at most m + k, so slot number + k is always free and none shares one (the Task 4 review); a drum's trial, which goes first, can still put one off by a trial. Measured: no late repeat in 100 seeds at each of 2 to 16 conditions and 30% or 50% breaks, for every order. Cost: none found.
4. **"After the stimulus appears" is the trial having entered the state its table names** (`Counting.appears`; `taskd._appeared`; the PI, 2026-10-13, "Task names it, required"). The field is the table's, as he put it, but **required at load, not as a table is built**: a table a block counts by (`CORRECT_ONLY` and the like) only classifies outcomes and names none, the structure's own saying where its stimulus appears; `check_task` refuses a task's structure whose table names none (`undeclared-counting`) or a state it does not have (`bad-counting`). A break before that state is owed whatever `after_stimulus` says -- a class the table gives that outcome of its own still decides it, as `EVERY_TRIAL` relies on -- and the trial's row records `shown`, §16.4's "whether their display was shown". A structure with no table, or one naming none -- a test's -- counts every trial as shown. Cost: if the PI wants a block's table to say where its stimulus appears as well, a field the block's table reads first.
5. **A trial at the longest trial counts as `NO_FIXATION`** (`task.HANG_COUNTS_AS`; the PI: "a missed acquisition"): owed unless the table says otherwise for `no_fixation`, and spent in the flat run, so a run of N trials runs N, hangs included. The record still writes `hang` (§6.8), which `resume`, `health` and the page read. Cost: if he meant the record to say `no_fixation`, the row's string changes and so do three readers' hang counts.
6. **`run_trial`'s `max_frames` defaults to 300 s at the frame period** (`run.longest_trial_frames`, to the nearest frame), so `taskd` passes nothing and the simulator gets the same cap. Cost: none found; a test that gives its own `max_frames` keeps it.
7. **Drumming**: every outcome but a right one (`task.RIGHT`: a correct answer or a correct rejection, the pair `codes.py` marks correct; the controller's ruling on the engineering review's I1) brings the same condition straight back and pays nothing, whatever the table says; only a trial's first try is judged for a criterion (the PI's answer 1; *revised 2026-10-18: Task 12 -- a streak every try, a percentage every try unless set to first answers only*); its failed tries in a row are counted from the first, and the tenth puts a warning on the console (his answer 2); a drum's repeat does not count toward the repeat limit. `Block.drum` declares it; `Scheduler.drum("block" | "run" | "off")` switches it, *off* holding to the block's end over a declaring block, *block* ending with the block, *run* with the run, and a switch made once the block's end rule is met is the next block's (the engineering review's Minor 10). Cost: if a wrong answer under drumming should count as well as repeat, one line in `record`.
8. **The console's clear forgives one trial of a condition's debt, or the rest of it** (decision 5, read as moving past a trial still owed; *the rest*, the controller's ruling on the science review's D5), dropping a repeat of it waiting or a drum's trial, refused with a sentence where the open block has no such condition or it owes nothing. Cost: if the PI meant un-counting a counted trial, another command.
9. **The repeat limit is the table's** (`Counting.cap`; "when a task sets none"), counting a condition's failed repeats in a row and resetting when it pays (the PI's answer 5; *revised 2026-10-18: at any trial not shown again, answers paid or not, and the task's limit unless the block sets one -- Task 7's fix round*); past it the condition's remaining debt is forgiven ("the condition is moved past"), a `Shortfall` flagged as possible avoidance **only when every failed try in the row was the animal's**; with a lost tracker or a fault among them it is named `rig` and not flagged (the controller's ruling on the engineering review's M4). Cost: if the PI wants the flag whenever the animal's own failures alone would have reached the limit, one condition in `record`.
10. **Shortfalls are recorded on the run's end row** (`shortfalls`), never as rows of their own: wl-preproc reports any `runs.jsonl` row but a start or an end as a problem (its `wl_preproc/events/rigruns.py`, read 2026-10-11 through the worktree's `wl-preproc` link), and reads `.get` from the end row, so new fields are safe. A clear is also a `controls.jsonl` row. Each shortfall's `after_trial` names the trial it followed, `None` for a clear before the run's first. Cost: none found.
11. **Each trial's row gains `counting`, `shown`, `repeat_of`, `at_once`, `previous`, `drummed` and `drumming`.** wl-preproc's trial reader requires six named fields and takes the rest as they are (`wl_preproc/events/rigtrials.py`, `_FIELDS`, same reading). Reading them into the published dataset is an ask, written (`docs/pending-wl-preproc-amendments.md`, XC-365). `repeat_of` is the trial's `index` in its run, which equals the scheduler's own count of the run's draws. Cost: none found.
12. **The two console commands are `link.ClearOwed` (`clear`, with `rest`) and `link.Drum` (`drum`)**, decoded by the wire's own rules and built from the page's body by the same function (`serve._WIRE_KINDS`), and **routed through `Session._command` as two branches beside the pause**, outside its two welfare-listed parts; so a clear sent while paused is applied by `_hold`'s drain, which reaches `_command` too. The page sends them; `wlx console` sends only `--set` and `--stop`, as for every other control. Cost: none.
13. **Telemetry schema 16**: `Telemetry.shortfalls` (`ShortfallRow`s), `Telemetry.drumming`, `Telemetry.clearable`, and `owed` now naming every condition of the open block (it named only the queued ones, one under a block drawing with replacement). Cost: a console built for 15 refuses 16 by name, as every bump has.
14. **A scheduler with no table** (a test's) answers what every structure shares -- an answer counted, shown or not; the four of `REPEATED`, a hang and a break before the stimulus owed -- and raises on an outcome each structure declares (the answer first, the engineering review's Minor 6). A run's structure always has one (`check_task`). Cost: none.
15. **No compatibility names are kept** for the removed sets (call 2): the repository's tests were their only readers, and each is migrated in the task that removes them.
16. **The designed-order repeat rule (§16.4) and a repeat's drawn values (§16.5) wait for build C3**, which brings the orders and the drawn values they need (XC-361, XC-362); the once-a-second checks inside a long trial (§6.8) are the warnings build's XC-373.
17. **The holds' backstops are fixed numbers** (Task 10): `verify` bounded at 3 s, `hold_fix` and `calibration`'s `hold` at 10 s -- each longer than any legal hold, so a hold in progress is never cut off, and no longer than the setting that bounded the state before (`response_deadline`'s and `fix_acquisition`'s highest), so no trial lasts longer than it could; check 4 needs a bound, and no setting's range lies wholly above the holds'. The ruling named the four `verify` states; the warning found the fixation holds and calibration's hold cut short the same way, and they are fixed too. Cost: if the PI wants the bounds live-editable, a setting each, on the shared list.
18. **`hold-cut-short` reads declared ranges**: a plain `After` whose lowest value is below its state's `Hold`'s highest is flagged; one with a `since` (it starts later) and one naming the hold's own setting are not. Each setting is read through `_span`, as every range check is since the warnings build: a side with no bound reaches as far as it can, so a hold with no highest is warned against any plain timeout (the controller's pre-flight ruling of 2026-10-18; this call first read `check._ranges`, which the warnings build removed). A warning in every session kind. Because the warnings build re-runs the load-time checks with a range widened to a value outside it (`check.beyond_range`), a hold set past its range can raise it as a new warning there too -- `fix_hold` at 8 s against a `fix_acquisition` from 0.5 s, which its test now expects (Task 10). Cost: a task bounding a hold by a `since` timeout is not warned.
19. **A correct rejection is right for a criterion and a condition's correct count** (`task.RIGHT`), fixed here beside drumming rather than filed (the engineering review's I1 named the gap): `codes.py` and the PI's rollup of 2026-09-26 already count it correct. Cost: none found; no reference task produces one yet.
20. **The page offers *clear* only where a trial of the open block can be owed** (`Scheduler.clearable`, `Telemetry.clearable`; the engineering review's Minor 3): the table owes some outcome, or trials are drummed. Cost: none.
21. **The review report shows each structure's counting table** (the engineering review's Minor 8): every outcome's class after the stimulus and before, the hang's, withholding and the limit, under "What a reviewer confirms". Cost: none.
22. **The hang's and the drum's warnings go on the console's changes feed only** (`Session._feed`, as the warnings build's live warnings do), not into `controls.jsonl` or `warnings.jsonl`: the trial rows already record the hang and every drummed try, and nothing is accepted. Cost: if the PI wants each in the record, one `_control` call each.
23. **`check.TRIAL_CAP` is derived from `run.LONGEST_TRIAL_S`** (Task 5; the controller's addendum on the warnings build's collision): `check` imports it from `run`, which imports only `task`, so there is no cycle; the warnings build's test that pinned `run_trial`'s old default now pins the sentence to the constant. Cost: none.
24. **A broken target hold in the detection tasks strobes `OUTCOME_TARGET_BREAK` (4120)** with its `TARGET_BREAK` (Task 10), as `visual_search`'s does: the allocation marks every break `TRIAL_ABORT`, and the reason code is how an analysis tells which (`codes.py`). Their `WRONG_TARGET` strobed no reason code. Cost: none found; 4120 is allocated.

25. **The revision of 2026-10-18's calls** are Appendix B's items 14 to 22, each with what it costs if wrong: how a streak is told from a percentage, `criterion_over` removed, what a criterion block draws past its targets and that it can run until a limit ends it, each training switch for its run, what "trials" counts and what still counts presentations, what "right first time" means, and that a trial used up ends a trial's count as an answer does.

## Global Constraints

- US English in code, comments and docs (CLAUDE.md).
- **The PI's sixteen decisions of 2026-10-18, and his answer of that day "Judge its first answer"**, as above, each quoted where it is built (Task 7's and 8's fix rounds, Tasks 12 to 15, and Tasks 9 and 11 as revised); where they refine an earlier answer the earlier one is kept, marked.
- **The PI's eight decisions of 2026-10-11, seven answers of 2026-10-12 and answers to Appendix B of 2026-10-13, as above**, each quoted where it is built: each table names the step its stimulus appears in ("Task names it, required"); leaving the target early a target break in both detection tasks ("Accept, target break"); repeats "at a random slot within the next k trials", in every block ("Same rule, within the next k"), k "At least 3", never the very next; drumming, "a training tool ... where a trial is repeated until correct", "switched on per block or run by the trainer", recorded, its repeats left out of the criterion and a warning "after 10 failed tries in a row"; the cap's trial "is a missed acquisition, not a fixation break", repeated like a fault, the cap "derived from 300 s at the session's rate"; a break after the stimulus appears has "no default; each task declares", refused at load when undeclared; the repeat cap "no limit (repeat until done)", counting "Tries in a row of one trial", with "some way in the console to manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count", recorded; "No response" in both detection tasks a miss, which steps the staircase up; calibration "No limit"; "balanced" within each run (C2b's); warnings over refusals.
- **Counting** (engine spec §16.1): "One table classifies every outcome, per trial structure: whether it counts toward its condition's target, and whether it is repeated. Counted by default: every trial with an answer: correct, wrong, early and late variants, and, where withholding is an answer (catch trials, detection, go/no-go), misses, correct rejections and false alarms. Not counted: trials that end before the decision (breaks, no fixation, faults)."
- **Repeats** (§16.2): "Repeated by default: `FIXATION_BREAK`, `NO_FIXATION`, `TRACKER_LOST`, `FAULT`. For other breaks the task chooses ... whether such a trial is spent (counted as presented and marked an abort) or owed (shown again later). Not repeated: wrong targets."
- **Placement** (§16.3): "at a random later point in its block, never the very next trial ... unless it is the only condition still owed, when it repeats at once, recorded", "later" now the PI's within-k rule for every block.
- **The realized sequence** (§16.4): "each trial's actual predecessor, aborted ones included, and whether their display was shown" is recorded.
- **The cap** (§16.6, as the PI read it on 2026-10-12): failed repeats in a row of one trial; "past N repeats in a block, the condition's remaining debt is forgiven, its shortfall recorded, shown and flagged as possible avoidance".
- **The longest trial** (§6.8): "The longest trial is 5 minutes (the loop's frame cap derived from 300 s at the session's rate); a trial reaching it is recorded as today's `"hang"` category, which `resume`, `health` and the page already read."
- **wl-preproc's readers**: `runs.jsonl` holds start and end rows only; a trial's line keeps the six fields wl-preproc requires (`index`, `subject`, `outcome`, `block`, `condition`, `params`), new fields beside them.
- Hot path: **nothing new per frame**. The table is read, a repeat placed, a warning fed and a row written between trials; `run_trial`'s cap is computed once per trial, before its first frame.
- No timing claim without a measurement (CLAUDE.md). The cap's frames and the holds' backstops are arithmetic on declared durations, not measurements of this system; the placement numbers are counts from a seeded probe, not timings.
- No new dependency. **No welfare-critical function changes** (Task 11 compares them byte for byte, and `Session._command` with its three new branches taken out: Task 8's two and Task 14's).
- Every new or changed function is proven able to fail (a named break, its test seen failing as `1 failed`, restored byte for byte, `__pycache__` cleared). **Read the harness's output, not its exit code** (CLAUDE.md). Never run the suite, edit a test or `git add` while a sweep is in flight. **A red step that is an import error is one by design** -- the task's tests import what the task adds -- and is said so where it occurs; its breaks are what prove the tests can fail.
- **The interpreter**: the build worktree's own `.superpowers/venv/bin/python` (written `$PY` below), set up as A2's was, **always with the shell's current directory at the build worktree, in the same call** (`cd <worktree> && $PY …`), never with `-I`, so the worktree's `wl_xcon` is the one imported (CHECKPOINT, "Learned"). Check once: `$PY -c "import wl_xcon; print(wl_xcon.__file__)"` names the worktree's. `WLX_REQUIRE_PREPROC=1` works through the worktree's `wl-preproc` link (git ignores it).
- The suite: `WLX_REQUIRE_PREPROC=1 WLX_REQUIRE_BROWSER=1 PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 $PY -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` (`-n auto` where `pytest-xdist` is installed, as it is in `engine-a2`'s environment). One file: `$PY -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/<file>`. A red step's expected output is for its command as written, serially; where `-n auto` prints more (it does not stop at a collection error), both are given.
- **How the code steps are written**: each edit is an exact text in a named file -- "after", "before", "replace ... with", "delete" -- copied from the run that verified the plan. Every anchor occurs once in its file at the base (Task 1's) or after the tasks before it; an anchor that does not is a sign the base moved, and the controller fixes the anchor, never the code. An inserted block keeps the blank lines PEP 8 puts around it (two between top-level definitions, one between methods), which the steps' code blocks do not show at their edges. New lines keep to 100 columns (XC-182's lint).
- **A test file's new imports go into its import block at the top**, merged with what it already imports; every step that adds tests says which names to import.
- **Every citation in a doc's prose names a key `docs/references/library.bib` has** at that commit (`tests/test_references.py`). This plan adds no key: each it cites (`nimhmonkeylogic2026task`, `stanislaw1999calculation`, `levitt1971transformed`, `luo2015neuronal`, `bichot2002priming`, `westerberg2020priming`) is in the library, for a claim decisions.md already records it for.
- `YYYY-MM-DD` in a step is the day that step runs. Backlog IDs XC-361 to XC-365 and XC-367 and decisions TSK-52 to TSK-56 are this plan's; paste them as written.

## Review Focus

The five inputs most likely to bite a person running a session that no task's own tests would otherwise exercise, each pinned by a test in its owning task:

1. **A long blink during the fixation hold, in a task that uses up breaks after the stimulus** (`visual_search`). Expected: owed, shown again like a fixation break, never "counted as presented" when nothing was presented; the row says `shown: false`. Tests: Task 1, `test_a_break_before_the_stimulus_appeared_is_owed_whatever_the_structure_says_of_breaks`; Task 3, `test_a_break_before_the_stimulus_appeared_is_owed_even_where_breaks_are_spent` and `test_each_trial_row_says_what_it_did_to_its_count_and_whether_its_stimulus_appeared`.
2. **A block drawing with replacement, or constrained, with breaks every few trials, and a block of two conditions.** Expected: no repeat is ever the very next trial unless nothing else could be drawn, none comes later than its k, a two-condition block's repeat comes two or three trials later, and a constrained block's runs stay within their limit while two conditions are owed. Tests: Task 4, `test_a_repeat_comes_back_within_the_next_k_trials_and_never_the_very_next` (three orders, three seeds), `test_no_repeat_waits_past_its_k_when_breaks_come_often`, `test_in_a_block_of_two_conditions_a_repeat_comes_two_or_three_trials_later`, `test_a_constrained_block_keeps_its_runs_short_while_two_conditions_are_owed`, `test_a_waiting_repeat_comes_early_rather_than_let_a_constrained_run_grow`.
3. **A trial that hangs until the cap, in a block and in a plain run of N.** Expected: in the block, owed and shown again (at once when its condition is the only one owed), its line still `hang`; in the plain run, one of the N; either way a warning on the changes feed naming the trial. Tests: Task 3, `test_a_trial_at_the_longest_trial_is_owed_and_in_a_flat_run_pays` and the revised `test_a_trial_that_reaches_no_outcome_is_still_closed`; Task 4, `test_a_trial_that_reaches_the_longest_trial_is_shown_again_as_a_missed_acquisition`; Task 5, `test_a_trial_that_reaches_the_longest_trial_ends_there_with_no_outcome` and `test_a_trial_at_the_longest_trial_is_said_on_the_console`.
4. **A trainer pauses, clears a trial or the rest of a condition, and resumes.** Expected: the clear is applied while paused -- `_hold` drains it into `Session._command`, beside the pause, outside its listed parts -- recorded between the pause and the resume, and the run completes; *clear the rest* leaves the condition owing nothing and its shortfall the rest it owed. Tests: Task 8, `test_a_clear_sent_while_paused_is_applied_at_once`, `test_a_person_clears_the_rest_of_a_condition_and_it_is_recorded`.
5. **Drumming over catch trials, with a criterion, failing on and on, and switched as a block ends.** Expected: a drummed catch condition finishes on correct rejections; a streak judges every try in order, a percentage every try unless set to first answers only (revised 2026-10-18); ten failed tries in a row put one warning on the feed and drumming goes on; a switch drained at a block's end is the next block's. Tests: Task 6, `test_a_drummed_catch_condition_finishes_on_correct_rejections`, `test_a_drummed_trial_that_fails_ten_tries_in_a_row_is_said_on_the_console`; Task 12, `test_a_streak_under_drumming_judges_every_answer_in_order`, `test_a_percentage_under_drumming_judges_every_answer_unless_set_to_first_answers`, `test_only_a_trials_first_answer_is_judged_for_a_percentage_under_the_first_answers_setting`; Task 8, `test_a_drumming_switch_sent_as_a_block_ends_is_for_the_next_block`.
6. **A drummed training block on a streak, past its targets, in a piloting session, with *clear* switched on mid-run** (the revision of 2026-10-18). Expected: the block ends only once the streak is met and every target is used up, drawing every condition past its targets and drumming there too; every row lists the aids on; *clear* refused with its sentence until switched on, then applied and recorded with who, its count starting over; the run's end row carries each condition's trials and presentations, a drummed trial one trial. Tests: Task 13, `test_past_its_targets_a_criterion_block_draws_every_condition_until_the_criterion_is_met`, `test_a_drummed_criterion_block_goes_on_drumming_past_its_targets`; Task 14, `test_clear_is_refused_until_a_trainer_switches_it_on_and_each_row_marks_the_aids_on`, `test_a_blocks_drumming_works_in_a_piloting_session_and_its_rows_mark_it`; Task 8's fix round, `test_clearing_one_trial_starts_its_conditions_failed_tries_over`; Task 15, `test_a_runs_end_row_records_each_conditions_counts_a_drummed_trial_one_trial`.

### Task 1: The counting table

Engine spec §16.1-16.2 as a declaration: what every outcome does to its condition's count. Fixes, at the table, the first two defects: correct rejections and false alarms are answers (`scheduler.RESPONDED` left them out), and a target break and a blink break follow what the structure declares (`scheduler.REQUEUED` repeated both whatever a task wanted). Four classes, the fourth, **unpaid**, for "run until ten correct": a wrong answer there pays nothing and is not shown again, as the PI ruled on 2026-08-31 and §16.2 says ("Not repeated: wrong targets"; call 2). One set of right answers, `RIGHT` -- a correct trial and a correct rejection, the pair `codes.py` marks correct -- which drumming, the criterion and the correct count read (Tasks 3 and 6). **The table names the state its stimulus appears in** (`appears`; the PI, 2026-10-13, "Task names it, required": "Each task's counting table names the step where its stimulus appears, and the load check refuses a table without one. A cued task names the cue step."), which Task 2's load check requires of a task's structure and Task 3 reads. The scheduler reads the table from Task 3.

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/task.py` (a section before `class Task`; `Trial.counting`)
- Create: `tests/test_counting.py`
- Modify (test): `tests/test_declarations.py` (`DEFAULTS`; one hash test)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `task.Outcome` (unchanged).
- Produces: `COUNTED = "counted"`, `SPENT = "spent"`, `OWED = "owed"`, `UNPAID = "unpaid"`, `COUNTING_CLASSES`; `RIGHT`, `ANSWERS`, `REPEATED`, `AFTER_STIMULUS: frozenset[Outcome]`, the last three with `Outcome.NO_RESPONSE` partitioning `Outcome`, and `RIGHT` inside `ANSWERS`; `HANG_COUNTS_AS = Outcome.NO_FIXATION`; `Counting(after_stimulus: str, appears: str | None = None, withholding: bool = False, outcomes: dict[str, str] = {}, cap: int | None = None)`, frozen, refused as it is built when malformed, and `Counting.of(outcome: Outcome | None, shown: bool = True) -> str`; `Trial.counting: Counting | None = None`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/test_counting.py`:

```python
"""Each trial structure's counting table (engine spec §16.1-16.2; engine build C2a).

What every outcome does to its condition's count: counted, an answer; spent, used up and marked an
abort; owed, shown again; unpaid, neither paid nor shown again. Declared by the structure, never
decided by the scheduler, because "run until ten correct" and "show the array a hundred times"
are both ordinary designs (TSK-26).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from wl_xcon.codes import _standing_outcomes
from wl_xcon.task import (
    AFTER_STIMULUS,
    ANSWERS,
    COUNTED,
    HANG_COUNTS_AS,
    OWED,
    REPEATED,
    RIGHT,
    SPENT,
    UNPAID,
    Counting,
    Outcome,
)


def test_every_outcome_falls_under_exactly_one_rule():
    """Total and disjoint, as `_FAMILY` is: an outcome added to the vocabulary without a rule here
    would be classed by nothing anyone decided."""
    rules = [ANSWERS, REPEATED, AFTER_STIMULUS, frozenset({Outcome.NO_RESPONSE})]

    assert frozenset().union(*rules) == frozenset(Outcome)
    assert sum(len(rule) for rule in rules) == len(Outcome)


def test_the_right_answers_are_the_two_the_codes_mark_correct():
    """`codes.py`: "A correct rejection *is* a correct trial". Drumming ends, a criterion judges and
    a condition's correct count counts on this one set (the engine C2a plan's call 7)."""
    marked = _standing_outcomes()

    assert RIGHT == {o for o in Outcome if marked[o] == marked[Outcome.CORRECT]}
    assert RIGHT == {Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.CORRECT_REJECT} and RIGHT <= ANSWERS


def test_every_answer_counts_a_correct_rejection_and_a_false_alarm_among_them():
    """§16.1; TSK-27: where withholding is an answer, a correct rejection and a false alarm are
    answers. `scheduler.RESPONDED` left both out, so a block of catch trials never met its
    target (the engine C2a plan's first defect)."""
    for after in (SPENT, OWED):
        table = Counting(after_stimulus=after)
        assert {table.of(outcome) for outcome in ANSWERS} == {COUNTED}
    assert {Outcome.CORRECT_REJECT, Outcome.FALSE_ALARM} <= ANSWERS


def test_the_four_that_end_before_the_decision_are_owed_whatever_the_structure_says_of_breaks():
    """§16.2; TSK-29: a fixation break, no fixation, a lost tracker and a fault are repeated by
    default, so the condition still gets its datum."""
    assert REPEATED == {Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.NO_FIXATION, Outcome.TRACKER_LOST,
                        Outcome.FAULT}
    for after in (SPENT, OWED):
        table = Counting(after_stimulus=after)
        assert {table.of(outcome) for outcome in REPEATED} == {OWED}


@pytest.mark.parametrize("after", [SPENT, OWED])
def test_a_target_break_and_a_blink_break_follow_what_the_structure_declares(after):
    """§16.2's "other breaks"; the PI, 2026-10-11: no default, each task declares. `REQUEUED`
    repeated a target break and a blink break whatever a task said (the plan's second defect)."""
    table = Counting(after_stimulus=after)

    assert {table.of(outcome) for outcome in AFTER_STIMULUS} == {after}
    assert {Outcome.TARGET_BREAK, Outcome.BLINK_BREAK} <= AFTER_STIMULUS


def test_a_break_before_the_stimulus_appeared_is_owed_whatever_the_structure_says_of_breaks():
    """A long blink during the fixation hold ends the trial as a blink break before anything was
    presented: owed, as a fixation break is, never "counted as presented" (§16.1: "Not counted:
    trials that end before the decision"). An answer stays an answer, and a line of the table's
    own for the outcome still decides it."""
    table = Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT)
    declared = Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT, outcomes={"blink_break": SPENT})

    assert table.of(Outcome.BLINK_BREAK, shown=False) == OWED
    assert table.of(Outcome.BLINK_BREAK, shown=True) == SPENT
    assert table.of(Outcome.CORRECT, shown=False) == COUNTED
    assert declared.of(Outcome.BLINK_BREAK, shown=False) == SPENT


def test_no_response_is_a_miss_where_withholding_is_an_answer_and_a_break_where_it_is_not():
    """§16.1; the PI, 2026-10-11: "No response" in both detection tasks is a miss, counted."""
    assert Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, withholding=True).of(Outcome.NO_RESPONSE) == COUNTED
    assert Counting(after_stimulus=OWED).of(Outcome.NO_RESPONSE) == OWED
    assert Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT).of(Outcome.NO_RESPONSE) == SPENT


def test_a_trial_at_the_longest_trial_counts_as_a_missed_acquisition():
    """The PI, 2026-10-11 (XC-287): "it isn't a fixation break, it is a missed acquisition" --
    repeated like a fault, by `NO_FIXATION`'s class, whatever the structure says of it."""
    assert HANG_COUNTS_AS is Outcome.NO_FIXATION
    assert Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT).of(None) == OWED
    spent = Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, outcomes={"no_fixation": SPENT})
    assert spent.of(None) == spent.of(Outcome.NO_FIXATION) == SPENT


def test_a_class_the_structure_declares_for_an_outcome_overrides_the_rules():
    """TSK-26: a block training fixation may count a fixation break as an error; "run until ten
    correct" leaves a wrong answer unpaid, neither paid nor shown again (§16.2: "Not repeated:
    wrong targets")."""
    table = Counting(after_stimulus=OWED,
                     outcomes={"fixation_break": COUNTED, "wrong_target": UNPAID})

    assert table.of(Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK) == COUNTED
    assert table.of(Outcome.WRONG_TARGET) == UNPAID
    assert table.of(Outcome.CORRECT) == COUNTED


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("fields", "said"),
    [
        ({"after_stimulus": "repeated"}, "a break after the stimulus is spent"),
        ({"after_stimulus": UNPAID}, "a break after the stimulus is spent"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "appears": ""}, "names the state its stimulus appears in"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "appears": 3}, "names the state its stimulus appears in"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "withholding": "yes"}, "withholding is True or False"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "outcomes": [("correct", COUNTED)]}, "a dict of outcome names"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "outcomes": {"hang": OWED}}, "'hang' is not an outcome"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "outcomes": {"correct": "paid"}}, "'correct' is given 'paid'"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "cap": -1}, "a repeat cap is a whole number"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "cap": True}, "a repeat cap is a whole number"),
        ({"after_stimulus": OWED, "cap": 2.5}, "a repeat cap is a whole number"),
    ],
)
def test_a_malformed_counting_table_is_refused_as_it_is_built(fields, said):
    """Its shape is refused as it is built, as a `Task`'s is; what a task declares with it is
    checked at load (`check.check_task`)."""
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=said):
        Counting(**fields)


def test_a_counting_table_says_what_breaks_after_the_stimulus_do_or_cannot_be_built():
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: "no default; each task declares"."""
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match="after_stimulus"):
        Counting()
    assert Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT, cap=0).cap == 0


def test_a_counting_table_names_the_state_its_stimulus_appears_in():
    """The PI, 2026-10-13, "Task names it, required": "Each task's counting table names the step
    where its stimulus appears ... A cued task names the cue step." A table a block counts by
    (`scheduler.CORRECT_ONLY`) names none: where the stimulus appears is the structure's, and
    `check.check_task` refuses a structure whose table names none."""
    assert Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, appears="cue").appears == "cue"
    assert Counting(after_stimulus=OWED).appears is None
```

def test_a_structures_counting_table_is_part_of_the_tasks_content():
    """Engine build C2a: what a break after the stimulus does changes what a run's count means, so
    it moves the hash; a structure that declares none hashes as before the field existed."""
    plain = _task(HOLD)
    trial = plain.structures[plain.default]
    spent = dataclasses.replace(trial, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT))
    owed = dataclasses.replace(trial, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED))

    def of(structure):
        return content_hash(Task({plain.default: structure}, default=plain.default))

    assert len({of(trial), of(spent), of(owed)}) == 3
    assert of(trial) == content_hash(plain)

`wl_xcon/task.py`: insert

```python
# --- Counting and repeats (engine spec §16; engine build C2a) ---------------------------------
#
# What each trial does to its condition's count, declared by each trial structure (§16.1: "One
# table classifies every outcome, per trial structure"), so the scheduler never decides an
# experimental design: "run until ten correct" and "show the array a hundred times" are both
# ordinary (TSK-26).

#: What a trial does to its condition's count (engine spec §16.1-16.2): **counted**, an answer,
#: paying the condition's debt; **spent**, used up -- it pays the debt as a presentation and is
#: marked an abort; **owed**, shown again later, paying nothing; **unpaid**, paying nothing and
#: not shown again -- the block's order draws the condition as it would have -- which is what
#: "run until ten correct" does with a wrong answer (the PI, 2026-08-31: "wrong choice not"
#: re-queued; §16.2: "Not repeated: wrong targets").
COUNTED = "counted"
SPENT = "spent"
OWED = "owed"
UNPAID = "unpaid"
COUNTING_CLASSES = (COUNTED, SPENT, OWED, UNPAID)

#: The right answers: a correct trial, and a correct rejection, which `codes.py` marks correct
#: too ("A correct rejection *is* a correct trial"). What ends a drum, and what a criterion and a
#: condition's correct count count as right (`scheduler.Scheduler.record`).
RIGHT = frozenset({Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.CORRECT_REJECT})

#: The answers, counted in every structure (§16.1; TSK-27): the responses to the target and to a
#: distractor, early and late ones too, and a correct rejection and a false alarm, which a task
#: produces only where withholding is an answer.
ANSWERS = frozenset(
    {
        Outcome.CORRECT,
        Outcome.EARLY_RESPONSE,
        Outcome.LATE_RESPONSE,
        Outcome.WRONG_TARGET,
        Outcome.EARLY_ERROR,
        Outcome.LATE_ERROR,
        Outcome.CORRECT_REJECT,
        Outcome.FALSE_ALARM,
    }
)

#: Ending before the decision, the animal not engaging or the rig failing: owed by default (§16.2;
#: TSK-29), so the condition still gets its datum.
REPEATED = frozenset(
    {Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.NO_FIXATION, Outcome.TRACKER_LOST, Outcome.FAULT}
)

#: The breaks after the stimulus appears (§16.2's "other breaks"): leaving the target, a catch
#: trial's or a motion's break, a long blink, and looking elsewhere (`ABORT`, which is no break but
#: ends a trial after the stimulus as one does). **No default** (the PI, 2026-10-11): each
#: structure says whether they are spent or owed (`Counting.after_stimulus`). One that ends a trial
#: before its stimulus appeared is owed, as a fixation break is, unless the table gives the outcome
#: a class of its own (`Counting.of`).
AFTER_STIMULUS = frozenset(
    {
        Outcome.TARGET_BREAK,
        Outcome.CATCH_BREAK,
        Outcome.MOTION_BREAK,
        Outcome.BLINK_BREAK,
        Outcome.ABORT,
    }
)

#: What a trial that reached the longest trial counts as (engine spec §6.8; `run.LONGEST_TRIAL_S`):
#: **a missed acquisition** (the PI, 2026-10-11: "it isn't a fixation break, it is a missed
#: acquisition"), `NO_FIXATION`'s class -- owed unless the structure says otherwise, so it is
#: repeated like a fault. Its record still says `hang` (§6.8), which `resume`, `health` and the
#: page read.
HANG_COUNTS_AS = Outcome.NO_FIXATION


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Counting:
    """One trial structure's counting table (engine spec §16.1-16.2): what each outcome does to its
    condition's count, as four declarations.

    - `after_stimulus`: whether a break after the stimulus appears (`AFTER_STIMULUS`, and no
      response where withholding is not an answer) is `SPENT` -- used up, counted as presented
      and marked an abort -- or `OWED`, shown again. **Required, with no default** (the PI,
      2026-10-11: "no default; each task declares"); `check.check_task` refuses a task's structure
      without a table.
    - `appears`: the state the stimulus appears in -- the target's or the array's, a cued task's
      cue (the PI, 2026-10-13: "Task names it, required") -- so a break before the trial reached
      it ended the trial before anything was presented. `check.check_task` refuses a task's
      structure whose table names none, or a state it does not have; a table a block counts by
      (`scheduler.Block.counting`) names none, the structure's own saying where its stimulus
      appears.
    - `withholding`: whether not answering is an answer here (detection, catch trials, go/no-go), so
      no response is a miss, counted (§16.1; the PI, 2026-10-11, for both detection tasks).
    - `outcomes`: an outcome's class where it differs from those rules, by its wire string
      (`Outcome.value`): a training block counting a fixation break as an error, or "run until ten
      correct" leaving every answer but a right one unpaid.
    - `cap`: how many failed repeats in a row of one trial of a condition are allowed before its
      remaining debt is forgiven, its shortfall recorded and shown (§16.6, as the PI read it on
      2026-10-12: "The condition is moved past after 3 failed repeats in a row, and the count
      resets once it is done"); `None`, no limit, repeated until done (the PI, 2026-10-11), with
      the console's *clear* to move past one by hand.

    Every answer (`ANSWERS`) is counted and every outcome of `REPEATED` owed, unless `outcomes` says
    otherwise; `of` reads the table."""

    after_stimulus: str
    appears: "str | None" = None
    withholding: bool = False
    outcomes: "dict[str, str]" = field(default_factory=dict)
    cap: "int | None" = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.after_stimulus not in (SPENT, OWED):
            raise ValueError(
                f"a break after the stimulus is {SPENT} (used up) or {OWED} (shown again), not "
                f"{self.after_stimulus!r}"
            )
        if self.appears is not None and (not isinstance(self.appears, str) or not self.appears):
            raise ValueError(
                f"a counting table's appears names the state its stimulus appears in, not "
                f"{self.appears!r}"
            )
        if not isinstance(self.withholding, bool):
            raise ValueError("a counting table's withholding is True or False")
        if not isinstance(self.outcomes, dict):
            raise ValueError("a counting table's outcomes are a dict of outcome names to classes")
        names = {outcome.value for outcome in Outcome}
        for name, kind in self.outcomes.items():
            if name not in names:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{name!r} is not an outcome; the outcomes are {', '.join(sorted(names))}"
                )
            if kind not in COUNTING_CLASSES:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"an outcome is {', '.join(COUNTING_CLASSES)}, and {name!r} is given {kind!r}"
                )
        if self.cap is not None and (
            isinstance(self.cap, bool) or not isinstance(self.cap, int) or self.cap < 0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                f"a repeat cap is a whole number of repeats, 0 or more, or None for no limit; not "
                f"{self.cap!r}"
            )

    def of(self, outcome: "Outcome | None", shown: bool = True) -> str:
        """What `outcome` does to its condition's count. `None`, a trial that reached the longest
        trial, counts as `HANG_COUNTS_AS`. `shown` is whether the trial's stimulus had appeared
        (`taskd`: the trial entered the state the structure's table names, `appears`): a break
        before it ended the trial before anything was presented, so it is owed, as a fixation
        break is, whatever `after_stimulus` says -- though a class `outcomes` gives the outcome
        still decides it."""
        if outcome is None:
            outcome = HANG_COUNTS_AS
        declared = self.outcomes.get(outcome.value)
        if declared is not None:
            return declared
        if outcome in ANSWERS or (outcome is Outcome.NO_RESPONSE and self.withholding):
            return COUNTED
        if outcome in REPEATED or not shown:
            return OWED
        return self.after_stimulus
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Prove they can fail**

- `ANSWERS` without `Outcome.CORRECT_REJECT` and `Outcome.FALSE_ALARM`: `test_every_outcome_falls_under_exactly_one_rule` fails (the rules no longer cover every outcome).
- `RIGHT = frozenset({Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.CORRECT_REJECT})` → `RIGHT = frozenset({Outcome.CORRECT})`: `test_the_right_answers_are_the_two_the_codes_mark_correct` fails.
- `Counting.of`'s `if outcome in REPEATED or not shown:` → `if outcome in REPEATED:`: `test_a_break_before_the_stimulus_appeared_is_owed_whatever_the_structure_says_of_breaks` fails.
- `of`'s `declared = self.outcomes.get(outcome.value)` → `declared = None`: `test_a_class_the_structure_declares_for_an_outcome_overrides_the_rules` fails.
- `of` without its first two lines (`if outcome is None: outcome = HANG_COUNTS_AS`): `test_a_trial_at_the_longest_trial_counts_as_a_missed_acquisition` fails.
- `(outcome is Outcome.NO_RESPONSE and self.withholding)` → `(False)`: `test_no_response_is_a_miss_where_withholding_is_an_answer_and_a_break_where_it_is_not` fails.
- `__post_init__`'s `if self.after_stimulus not in (SPENT, OWED):` → `if False:`: `test_a_malformed_counting_table_is_refused_as_it_is_built[fields0-…]` fails.
- `__post_init__`'s `if self.appears is not None and (not isinstance(self.appears, str) or not self.appears):` → `if False:`: `test_a_malformed_counting_table_is_refused_as_it_is_built[fields2-…]` fails.
