```python
# --- the repeat cap (engine spec §16.6) ----------------------------------------------


def _breaking_on(cap, breaks: int, outcomes=(Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK,),
                 target: int = 3) -> Scheduler:
    """One condition owing `target` trials, its table's limit `cap`, its trials ending as
    `outcomes` say in turn, `breaks` of them."""
    scheduler = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=target)])],
                          seed=1, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, cap=cap))
    for n in range(breaks):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcomes[n % len(outcomes)])
    return scheduler


def test_past_its_cap_a_conditions_debt_is_forgiven_and_flagged_as_possible_avoidance():
    """§16.6: "past N repeats in a block, the condition's remaining debt is forgiven, its shortfall
    recorded, shown and flagged as possible avoidance". Two repeats allowed: the third break in a
    row forgives the three trials still owed, and the block can end."""
    scheduler = _breaking_on(cap=2, breaks=3)

    assert scheduler.requeued == ["a", "a"] and scheduler.counts("a").repeats == 2
    assert scheduler.shortfalls == [Shortfall("b", "a", 3, CAP, 2)]
    assert scheduler.shortfalls[0].possible_avoidance
    assert scheduler.owed("a") == 0 and scheduler.finished


def test_with_no_cap_a_condition_is_repeated_until_done():
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: with no cap set, "no limit (repeat until done)"; the console's clear
    moves past one by hand."""
    scheduler = _breaking_on(cap=None, breaks=50)

    assert scheduler.counts("a").repeats == 50 and scheduler.shortfalls == []
    assert scheduler.owed("a") == 3 and not scheduler.finished


def test_a_cap_of_zero_forgives_at_the_first_owed_trial():
    """A cap of 0: no repeat at all; the first owed trial forgives the debt."""
    scheduler = _breaking_on(cap=0, breaks=1)

    assert scheduler.requeued == [] and [s.trials for s in scheduler.shortfalls] == [3]


def test_the_repeat_limit_counts_failed_repeats_in_a_row_and_resets_when_the_condition_pays():
    """The PI, 2026-10-12: "Tries in a row of one trial" -- "The condition is moved past after 3
    failed repeats in a row, and the count resets once it is done." Counted over the block, a limit
    of 2 flagged 73% of conditions at an ordinary 20% break rate with a cap of 3 (the science
    review's O1); here two breaks, a correct trial, two breaks and a correct trial are four
    repeats and no shortfall, and only a third failure in a row forgives."""
    run = (Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.CORRECT)
    paid = _breaking_on(cap=2, breaks=6, outcomes=run, target=5)
    moved_past = _breaking_on(cap=2, breaks=6, outcomes=run + run[:2] + run[:1], target=5)

    assert (paid.counts("a").repeats, paid.shortfalls, paid.owed("a")) == (4, [], 3)
    assert moved_past.shortfalls == [Shortfall("b", "a", 4, CAP, 5)]


def test_a_shortfall_the_rig_made_is_named_for_the_rig_and_not_flagged_as_avoidance():
    """The controller's ruling on the engineering review's M4: only the animal's outcomes are
    flagged as possible avoidance. A limit reached with tries a lost tracker or a fault ended is
    named for the rig, and a camera that keeps dropping a condition is not called the animal's."""
    rig = _breaking_on(cap=1, breaks=2, outcomes=(Outcome.TRACKER_LOST,))
    mixed = _breaking_on(cap=1, breaks=2, outcomes=(Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.FAULT))
    animal = _breaking_on(cap=1, breaks=2, outcomes=(Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.NO_FIXATION))

    assert [(s.because, s.possible_avoidance) for s in rig.shortfalls] == [(RIG, False)]
    assert [(s.because, s.possible_avoidance) for s in mixed.shortfalls] == [(RIG, False)]
    assert [(s.because, s.possible_avoidance) for s in animal.shortfalls] == [(CAP, True)]


def test_a_wrong_answer_left_unpaid_never_feeds_the_repeat_limit():
    """The controller's ruling on the engineering review's I3: under "until N correct" a wrong
    answer is not placed as a repeat, so a condition the animal keeps getting wrong is neither
    forgiven nor flagged as avoidance."""
    table = Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, outcomes={"wrong_target": UNPAID}, cap=0)
    scheduler = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=2)])], seed=1,
                          counting=table)
    for _ in range(5):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET)

    assert (scheduler.shortfalls, scheduler.requeued, scheduler.owed("a")) == ([], [], 2)
```

```python
def test_a_runs_end_row_records_each_shortfall_its_cap_forgave(tmp_path):
    """Engine spec §16.6: the shortfall is recorded, on the run's end row, flagged as possible
    avoidance. A block whose own table allows no repeat forgives a condition's debt at its first
    owed trial: the row names the block, the condition, the trials forgiven and the trial after
    which they were, an owed one."""
    table = Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, withholding=True, cap=0)
    block = Block(name="b", counting=table,
                  conditions=[Condition(n, {"target_x": x}, target=10)
                              for n, x in (("near", 5.0), ("far", 12.0))])
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[block]))

    session.run()

    (end,) = [row for row in _runs(session) if row["event"] == "end"]
    rows = {row["index"]: row for row in _trial_rows(session)}
    assert end["shortfalls"], "the seed breaks on a condition"
    for short in end["shortfalls"]:
        assert (short["block"], short["because"], short["possible_avoidance"], short["by"]) == (
            "b", "cap", True, None)
        assert rows[short["after_trial"]]["condition"] == short["condition"]
        assert rows[short["after_trial"]]["counting"] == "owed"
    assert session.stopped_because == "every block is finished"
```

```python
    was repeated (`repeats`; a drum's are not); its failed repeats in a row since it last paid
    (`in_a_row`, which the repeat limit counts: §16.6 as the PI read it on 2026-10-12) and how
    many of those failed tries the rig lost (`rig`); and trials of its debt forgiven, by the limit
    or by a person (`forgiven`)."""

    attempted: int = 0
    completed: int = 0
    correct: int = 0
    spent: int = 0
    repeats: int = 0
    in_a_row: int = 0
    rig: int = 0
    forgiven: int = 0


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Shortfall:
    """Trials of a condition's debt forgiven in a block, recorded and shown (engine spec §16.6):
    `because` its repeat limit was reached by the animal's own failures (`CAP`), which is **flagged
    as possible avoidance**; or reached with tries the rig lost among them (`RIG`), named so and
    never flagged; or a person cleared them from the console (`CLEARED`, by `by`). `after_trial`,
    the number of the trial it happened after."""

    block: str
    condition: str
    trials: int
    because: str
    after_trial: int
    by: object = None

    @property
    def possible_avoidance(self) -> bool:
        """§16.6: a condition the animal itself failed past its limit is flagged as possible
        avoidance; one the rig failed on is not (the engine C2a plan's engineering review, M4)."""
        return self.because == CAP
```

```python
            else:

                counts.in_a_row += 1
                self.requeued.append(condition)

    def _forgive(self, condition: str, trials: int, because: str, by: object = None) -> None:
        """Forgive `trials` of `condition`'s debt in this block (§16.6): no repeat of it waits any
        longer, nor a drum's trial, and the shortfall is the run's record."""
        self._counts[condition].forgiven += trials
        self._due = [due for due in self._due if due[2] != condition]
        if self._drum is not None and self._drum[0] == condition:
            self._drum = None
        self.shortfalls.append(
            Shortfall(self.block.name, condition, trials, because, self._number - 1, by))
```

Run the two files: `321 passed`. Then the whole suite: **3616 passed**.

- [ ] **Step 5: Prove they can fail**

- `record`'s `if cap is not None and counts.in_a_row >= cap:` → `if False:`: `test_past_its_cap_a_conditions_debt_is_forgiven_and_flagged_as_possible_avoidance` fails.
- `record` without `counts.in_a_row += 1`: the same test fails (the limit never reached).
- `owed`'s `- counts.forgiven` dropped: the same test fails (the debt still owed).
- `record`'s `counts.in_a_row = counts.rig = 0` → `pass` (the count over the block, as drafted): `test_the_repeat_limit_counts_failed_repeats_in_a_row_and_resets_when_the_condition_pays` fails.
- `record`'s `RIG if counts.rig else CAP` → `CAP` (rig failures flagged as avoidance, the engineering review's M4): `test_a_shortfall_the_rig_made_is_named_for_the_rig_and_not_flagged_as_avoidance` fails; `counts.rig += outcome is not None and outcome.family is Family.RIG` → `counts.rig += False`: the same test fails.
- `record`'s `if kind == OWED and self.owed(condition) > 0:` → `if kind in (OWED, UNPAID) and self.owed(condition) > 0:`: `test_a_wrong_answer_left_unpaid_never_feeds_the_repeat_limit` fails.
- The end row's `"possible_avoidance": short.possible_avoidance,` → `"possible_avoidance": False,`: `test_a_runs_end_row_records_each_shortfall_its_cap_forgave` fails.

Body: §16.6, as the PI read it on 2026-10-12 (failed repeats in a row of one trial, the count reset once the condition pays); his decision 5 (no limit unless a task sets one); only the animal's outcomes flagged as possible avoidance, a limit reached with the rig's failures among the tries named for the rig (the engineering review's M4); a wrong answer left unpaid never feeding the limit (its I3); calls 9 and 10 (the end row, since wl-preproc reads only start and end rows); the red an import error by design; the count; the breaks seen failing.

---

### Task 8: Clearing a trial and switching drumming from the console

The PI's decision 5: "there should be some way in the console to manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count", recorded; and decision 2's switch, "by the trainer". Two commands on the wire, `ClearOwed` and `Drum`, decoded by the wire's rules; in the session, **two branches in `Session._command`, beside the pause and outside its two welfare-listed parts** (its `held` pass-through and its one `except` line), so a command drained while paused (`_hold`) reaches them too. A clear forgives one trial of the condition's debt in the open block, or **the rest of it** (the controller's ruling on the science review's D5: a refused condition owing 20 needed 20 clicks while it kept returning) (`Scheduler.clear`, call 8), refused with a sentence where there is nothing to clear; each is a `controls.jsonl` row with who sent it, and a clear is a shortfall on the run's end row. A drumming switch drained as a block ends is the next block's, and its row says so (Task 6; the engineering review's Minor 10). Build C3's change log takes both over with their reasons (XC-363).

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/link.py` (docstring; imports; `ClearOwed`, `Drum`; `Command`; `_encode_command`; `_command_from`)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/scheduler.py` (`Scheduler.clear`, before `drum`)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/taskd.py` (`Session._command`'s two branches; `Session._clear` and `_drum`, before `_ends`)
- Modify (test): `tests/test_link.py` (imports; two new), `tests/test_scheduler.py` (imports; three new), `tests/test_taskd.py` (imports; `_Drained`, `_near_far`; five new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Scheduler._forgive`, `CLEARED`, `Shortfall` (Task 7); `Scheduler.drum`, `drumming`, `DRUM_SCOPES` (Task 6); `Session._control`, `_refuse` (unchanged).
- Produces: `link.ClearOwed(condition: str, by: Actor, rest: bool = False)`, `KIND = "clear"`, wire `{"kind": "clear", "by", "condition", "rest"}`, `rest` true or false or refused; `link.Drum(scope: str, by: Actor)`, `KIND = "drum"`, wire `{"kind": "drum", "by", "scope"}`, `scope` one of `DRUM_SCOPES` or refused (`CommandRefused`); `Scheduler.clear(condition: str, by: object = None, rest: bool = False) -> str | None`, the refusal's sentence or `None`; `Session._clear(command, index)`, `Session._drum(command, index)`; `controls.jsonl` rows of kind `clear` (`condition`, `block`, `rest`, `cleared`, `owed`) and `drum` (`scope`, `block`, `was`, `now`).

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "command", [ClearOwed(condition="near", by=Box("jake")),
                ClearOwed(condition="near", by=Box("jake"), rest=True),
                Drum(scope="run", by=Box("jake"))],
    ids=["clear one", "clear the rest", "drum"])
def test_clearing_a_trial_and_switching_drumming_survive_the_wire_with_who_sent_them(command):
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: a console control to clear a trial from the count, and drumming switched
    by the trainer (engine build C2a)."""
    assert _decode_command(_encode_command(command)) == command
    assert (ClearOwed.KIND, Drum.KIND) == ("clear", "drum")


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("fields", "said"),
    [
        ({"kind": "clear", "condition": "", "by": JAKE}, "condition is text"),
        ({"kind": "clear", "by": JAKE}, "condition is text"),
        ({"kind": "clear", "condition": "near", "rest": "yes", "by": JAKE},
         "rest is true or false"),
        ({"kind": "drum", "scope": "on", "by": JAKE}, "block, run or off"),
        ({"kind": "drum", "by": JAKE}, "block, run or off"),
    ],
)
def test_a_malformed_clear_or_drum_is_refused_where_it_is_decoded(fields, said):
    with pytest.raises(CommandRefused) as refused:
        _decode_command(_packed(**fields))

    assert refused.value.name == fields["kind"]
    assert said in refused.value.why
```

```python
class _Drained(Simulated):
    """A link that queues `script[n]` as the `n`th drain begins: the commands reach the loop at
    the boundary before trial `n - 1`."""

    def __init__(self, script):
        super().__init__()
        self.script, self.drains = dict(script), 0

    def drain(self) -> list:
        self.drains += 1
        for command in self.script.get(self.drains, ()):
            self.queue(command)
        return super().drain()


def _near_far(**block) -> Block:
    return Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("near", {"target_x": 5.0}, target=4),
                                       Condition("far", {"target_x": 12.0}, target=4)], **block)


def test_a_person_clears_a_trial_from_a_conditions_count_and_it_is_recorded(tmp_path):
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: "there should be some way in the console to manually advance beyond or
    clear a specific trial in the count", recorded. The block owes `near` one trial fewer; the
    control row and the run's end row say who and where; a clear of a condition the block does not
    have is refused with the sentence, and changes nothing."""
    link = _Drained({3: [ClearOwed(condition="near", by=Box("jake")),
                         ClearOwed(condition="nowhere", by=Box("jake"))]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()]), link=link)

    session.run()

    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    assert sum(r["condition"] == "near" and r["counting"] != "owed" for r in rows) == 3
    assert sum(r["condition"] == "far" and r["counting"] != "owed" for r in rows) == 4
    (cleared,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "clear"]
    assert (cleared["condition"], cleared["block"], cleared["by"], cleared["trial_index"]) == (
        "near", "b", {"kind": "box", "name": "jake"}, 2)
    (end,) = [row for row in _runs(session) if row["event"] == "end"]
    assert [(s["condition"], s["trials"], s["because"], s["possible_avoidance"], s["by"],
             s["after_trial"]) for s in end["shortfalls"]] == [
        ("near", 1, "cleared", False, {"kind": "box", "name": "jake"}, 1)]
    assert [(name, why) for name, _, why in session.refusals] == [
        ("clear", "block 'b' has no condition 'nowhere', so nothing is cleared")]


def test_a_clear_sent_while_paused_is_applied_at_once(tmp_path):
    """The paused loop drains commands too (`_hold`), and a clear is applied there, before the
    resume: routed beside the pause, outside `Session._command`'s welfare-listed parts."""
    link = _Scripted(script={1: [ClearOwed(condition="near", by=Box("sam"))],
                             2: [Resume(by=Box("sam"))]}, step=1.0)
    link.queue(Pause(by=Box("jake")))
    session, wall = _walled(tmp_path, link, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()])
    link.wall = wall

    session.run()

    kinds = [row["kind"] for row in _controls_rows(session)]
    assert kinds[:3] == ["pause", "clear", "resume"]
    assert session.stop_kind == "completed"


def test_a_trainer_switches_drumming_on_for_the_run_and_every_row_says_so(tmp_path):
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: drumming switched on by the trainer, and recorded: a control row naming
    who switched it and to what, and every trial row from then on drawn while drumming."""
    link = _Drained({1: [Drum(scope="run", by=Box("jake"))]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()]), link=link)

    session.run()

    (switched,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "drum"]
    assert (switched["scope"], switched["was"], switched["now"], switched["by"]) == (
        "run", None, "run", {"kind": "box", "name": "jake"})
    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    assert {row["drumming"] for row in rows} == {"run"}
    assert all(row["drummed"] == (before["outcome"] != "correct")
               for before, row in zip(rows, rows[1:]))
    assert any(row["drummed"] for row in rows), "the seed gives the run a trial that is not correct"


def test_a_person_clears_the_rest_of_a_condition_and_it_is_recorded(tmp_path):
    """The science review's D5 and the controller's ruling: beside *clear one*, *clear the rest of
    this condition*, so a condition the animal refuses is moved past in one command, recorded as a
    clear is: its control row says how many trials it cleared, and the end row's shortfall."""
    link = _Drained({2: [ClearOwed(condition="far", by=Box("jake"), rest=True)]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()]), link=link)

    session.run()

    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    (cleared,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "clear"]
    (end,) = [row for row in _runs(session) if row["event"] == "end"]
    (short,) = end["shortfalls"]
    paid = sum(r["condition"] == "far" and r["counting"] in ("counted", "spent") for r in rows)
    assert (cleared["rest"], cleared["cleared"], cleared["owed"]) == (True, short["trials"], 0)
    assert (short["condition"], short["because"], short["trials"] + paid) == ("far", "cleared", 4)
    assert sum(r["condition"] == "near" and r["counting"] != "owed" for r in rows) == 4


def test_a_drumming_switch_sent_as_a_block_ends_is_for_the_next_block(tmp_path):
    """The engineering review's Minor 10: the loop drains commands before it advances, so a switch
    that arrives as a block's last trial pays was reset by the advance, its row naming the block
    that had ended. It is the next block's, and its row names that block."""
    blocks = [Block(name="A", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=1)], counting=EVERY_TRIAL),
              Block(name="B", conditions=[Condition("b", {}, target=3)])]
    link = _Drained({2: [Drum(scope="block", by=Box("jake"))]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=blocks), link=link)

    session.run()

    (switched,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "drum"]
    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    assert (switched["block"], switched["now"]) == ("B", "block")
    assert [row["drumming"] for row in rows if row["block"] == "A"] == [None]
    assert {row["drumming"] for row in rows if row["block"] == "B"} == {"block"}
```

```python
def test_a_cleared_trial_forgives_one_of_the_debt_and_drops_its_waiting_repeat():
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: "manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count".
    Clearing the trial a repeat is waiting for forgives one trial, records who did it, and the
    repeat never comes; a clear of nothing owed, or of no condition of the block, is refused with a
    sentence."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=2), Condition("b", {}, target=2)])
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)
    broke = scheduler.next_trial().name
    scheduler.record(broke, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)

    assert scheduler.clear(broke, by="jake") is None

    assert scheduler.owed(broke) == 1
    assert scheduler.shortfalls == [Shortfall("b", broke, 1, CLEARED, 0, "jake")]
    assert not scheduler.shortfalls[0].possible_avoidance
    draws = []
    while not scheduler.finished:
        scheduler.next_trial()
        draws.append(scheduler.drawn)
        scheduler.record(scheduler.drawn.condition.name, Outcome.CORRECT)
    assert all(draw.repeat_of is None for draw in draws) and len(draws) == 3
    assert scheduler.clear("c") == "block 'b' has no condition 'c', so nothing is cleared"
    assert scheduler.clear(broke) == f"'{broke}' owes nothing in block 'b', so nothing is cleared"


def test_clearing_a_drummed_trial_moves_past_it():
    """"Advance beyond": the drum's trial waiting to come back is dropped with the trial cleared."""
    scheduler = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=2)], drum=True)],
                          seed=1)
    scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET)

    scheduler.clear("a")
    scheduler.next_trial()

    assert not scheduler.drawn.drummed and scheduler.owed("a") == 1


def test_clearing_the_rest_of_a_condition_forgives_all_it_still_owes():
    """The science review's D5: a condition the animal refuses, owing many, is moved past at
    once."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=20),
                                        Condition("b", {}, target=1)])
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)

    assert scheduler.clear("a", by="jake", rest=True) is None

    assert scheduler.owed("a") == 0
    assert scheduler.shortfalls == [Shortfall("b", "a", 20, CLEARED, None, "jake")]
```

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ClearOwed:
    """**Clear one trial of a condition's debt** in the open block, or with `rest` all it still owes
    (the PI, 2026-10-11: "there should be some way in the console to manually advance beyond or
    clear a specific trial in the count"; *the rest*, the engine C2a plan's science review, D5): the
    condition owes one trial fewer, or none, a repeat of it waiting or a drum's trial waiting is
    dropped, and the run's end row records it as a shortfall with who cleared it (engine spec
    §16.6; `scheduler.Scheduler.clear`). `taskd` refuses it, with the sentence, where the block owes
    the condition nothing."""
```

```python
    def clear(self, condition: str, by: object = None, rest: bool = False) -> str | None:
        """Forgive one trial of `condition`'s debt in this block, or with `rest` all it still owes,
        as a person does from the console (the PI, 2026-10-11: "manually advance beyond or clear a
        specific trial in the count"; *the rest*, the engine C2a plan's science review, D5): a
        repeat of it waiting, or a drum's trial, is dropped, and the clearing is a `Shortfall`
        naming `by`. Returns why nothing was cleared -- the block has no such condition, or it owes
        nothing -- or `None`."""
        if condition not in self._counts:
            return (
                f"block {self.block.name!r} has no condition {condition!r}, so nothing is cleared"
            )
        owed = self.owed(condition)
        if owed == 0:
            return (
                f"{condition!r} owes nothing in block {self.block.name!r}, so nothing is cleared"
            )
        self._forgive(condition, owed if rest else 1, CLEARED, by)
        return None
```

```python
    def _clear(self, command, index: int) -> None:
        """**Clear one trial of a condition's debt, or the rest of it** (`link.ClearOwed`; the PI,
        2026-10-11: "manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count"), between
        trials or while paused: the open block's scheduler forgives it (`Scheduler.clear`), the
        run's end row records the shortfall with who cleared it, and `controls.jsonl` the control,
        how many it cleared and what the condition still owes. **Refused with the sentence**, and
        nothing changed, where the block owes the condition nothing."""
        scheduler = self._scheduler
        said = scheduler.clear(command.condition, by=command.by, rest=command.rest)
        if said is not None:
            self._refuse("clear", command.by, said)
            return
        cleared, owed = scheduler.shortfalls[-1].trials, scheduler.owed(command.condition)
        what = f"the rest, {cleared} trials," if command.rest else "one trial"
        self._control(
            "clear", command.by,
            f"cleared {what} of {command.condition} in block {scheduler.block.name}; it owes "
            f"{owed}",
            index, condition=command.condition, block=scheduler.block.name, rest=command.rest,
            cleared=cleared, owed=owed,
        )

    def _drum(self, command, index: int) -> None:
        """**Switch drumming** (`link.Drum`; the PI, 2026-10-11: "switched on per block or run by
        the trainer", and recorded): the scheduler drums from the next trial (`Scheduler.drum`),
        `controls.jsonl` records the switch with who made it, and every trial row says whether
        drumming was on as it was drawn. Engine build C3's change log takes it over, with its
        reason."""
        scheduler = self._scheduler
        was = scheduler.drumming
        # The block it is for: this one, or the next when this one's end rule is already met.
        block = scheduler.drum(command.scope)
        self._control(
            "drum", command.by, f"drumming {command.scope} in block {block}",
            index, scope=command.scope, block=block, was=was, now=scheduler.drumming,
        )
```

### Task 7, fix round 1: Any answer ends the trial, the task's limit holds in every block, and the review's points

**The resumed Task 7 implementer's brief** (the revision of 2026-10-18; on top of `505b78b`). Task 7's review (ledger, "Task 7: review Needs fixes") asked for fixes, and the PI settled its Important 1 on 2026-10-18; the same day he decided whose limit applies. This round builds both, and the review's Important 2 and minors 4 to 6:

- **Important 1, settled by the PI's decision 7** (asked: "Say condition A's trial breaks twice, then is answered wrong; later another trial of A breaks. Is that break failure 1 or failure 3?"): **"1: an answer ends the trial"** -- the repeat limit counts failed tries of one trial, and any answer, right or wrong, starts it over. `record` reset the count only when the condition paid, so under "until N correct" a break, an unpaid wrong answer and a break at a limit of 1 forgave the whole debt as possible avoidance. Now **any trial not shown again ends the trial**: an answer, paid or not, and a trial used up, which is not shown again either.
- **The PI's decision 8** (asked whose limit applies when a block counts by a table of its own): **"No: the task's limit applies unless the block sets its own"**. `record` took the limit from the block's table when it had one, so a named table (none sets a limit) removed the structure's. Now the block's table's limit when it sets one, else the structure's (`Scheduler._cap`). It is Task 7's code (the controller's ruling Q2 put the table choice in `_table`), so it is built here rather than in a task of its own.
- **Important 2**: the flag's each-try rule and the rig's reset had no test (two one-token mutants survived): a rig failure on the row's first try names the shortfall for the rig as surely as one on its last, and a trial that ends forgets the rig's failures with the rest of its count.
- **Minor 3** (the cap makes XC-364 likelier; name it in XC-364's line): **moot** -- the PI's decision 6 settles XC-364 (Task 13), and Task 11 files no line for it.
- **Minor 4**: `Counts.in_a_row`'s docstring was off by one: it counts a trial's failed tries in a row, its first try's included, each of which placed a repeat, not "failed repeats".
- **Minor 5**: `test_the_repeat_limit_counts_failed_repeats_in_a_row_and_resets_when_the_condition_pays`'s docstring contradicted itself ("a limit of 2 ... with a cap of 3", plan text); it now cites the science review's O1 without the figures, which this revision could not check.
- **The controller's ruling I3 on the revision's engineering review** (2026-10-18): the reset sits **above** `record`'s drum branch, which returns early, so a drummed answer ends the trial's count as well -- the PI's decision 7 says any answer. Its scenario is a fourth test: a limit of 1; `x` breaks; drumming on; `x`'s repeat answered wrong and drummed; drumming off; `x` breaks once -- failure 1 of a new trial, nothing forgiven. The suite count is one higher than the reset below the drum's branch gave.
- **Minor 6**: the ruling-3 test (`test_a_forgiven_conditions_waiting_repeat_is_not_drawn_and_none_is_placed_again`) recorded `a` three times with no draw between, a sequence no run makes. Its real consumer is a person's clear (Task 8), which forgives while a repeat waits; it now goes through `clear`.

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/scheduler.py` (`Counts`' docstring; `Scheduler._cap`, after `_table`; `record`'s docstring, its reset and its limit)
- Modify (test): `tests/test_scheduler.py` (one docstring; one test rewritten; four new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Scheduler._table`, `counting`, `_forgive`, `clear` (Tasks 3, 7, 8); `Counting.cap` (Task 1).
- Produces: `Scheduler._cap -> int | None`; `record` resets `Counts.in_a_row` and `Counts.rig` on any trial whose class is not `OWED`, before its drum branch, so a drummed answer resets them too.

```python
def test_a_forgiven_conditions_waiting_repeat_is_not_drawn_and_none_is_placed_again():
    """The placement guard (`record`: a repeat only while the condition owes anything) has its
    consumer: a limit of 1 lets the first failure place a repeat, which waits; the second
    failure, recorded before it is drawn, forgives the condition and takes the waiting repeat
    with it. A third result for the forgiven condition places nothing, and the draws that follow
    are the other condition's alone."""
    scheduler = Scheduler(
        [Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=3), Condition("b", {}, target=40)])],
        seed=1, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, cap=1))

    scheduler.record("a", Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)
    assert [due[2] for due in scheduler._due] == ["a"], "the first failure's repeat waits"
    scheduler.record("a", Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)
    scheduler.record("a", Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)
    drawn = []
    for _ in range(12):
        name = scheduler.next_trial().name
        drawn.append(name)
        scheduler.record(name, Outcome.CORRECT)

    assert scheduler._due == [] and scheduler.requeued == ["a"]
    assert set(drawn) == {"b"} and scheduler.owed("a") == 0
    assert [(s.condition, s.trials) for s in scheduler.shortfalls] == [("a", 3)]
```

```python
def test_a_forgiven_conditions_waiting_repeat_is_not_drawn_and_none_is_placed_again():
    """The placement guard (`record`: a repeat only while the condition owes anything) and
    `_forgive`'s dropping of a waiting repeat have their consumer in a person's clear: `a` breaks
    and its repeat waits; the rest of `a` is cleared, which takes the waiting repeat with it; the
    draws that follow are `b`'s alone, and a late result for `a` places nothing. (Task 7's review,
    minor 6: this test first recorded `a` three times with no draw between, which no run does.)"""
    scheduler = Scheduler(
        [Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=3), Condition("b", {}, target=40)])],
        seed=1, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED))
    for _ in range(5):
        name = scheduler.next_trial().name
        if name == "a":
            break
        scheduler.record(name, Outcome.CORRECT)
    else:
        raise AssertionError("the seed draws a within five trials")
    scheduler.record("a", Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)
    assert [due[2] for due in scheduler._due] == ["a"], "the break's repeat waits"

    assert scheduler.clear("a", rest=True) is None
    drawn = []
    for _ in range(12):
        name = scheduler.next_trial().name
        drawn.append(name)
        scheduler.record(name, Outcome.CORRECT)
    scheduler.record("a", Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)

    assert scheduler._due == [] and scheduler.requeued == ["a"]
    assert set(drawn) == {"b"} and scheduler.owed("a") == 0
    assert [(s.condition, s.trials, s.because) for s in scheduler.shortfalls] == [("a", 3, CLEARED)]
```

```python
def test_any_answer_ends_a_trial_so_its_conditions_next_trial_counts_its_failures_from_0():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "1: an answer ends the trial": the repeat limit counts failed tries of
    one trial, and any answer, right or wrong, starts it over. Under "until N correct" a wrong
    answer pays nothing, and the count went on through it: a break, a wrong answer and a break at a
    limit of 1 forgave the whole debt as possible avoidance (Task 7's review, Important 1)."""
    table = Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, outcomes={"wrong_target": UNPAID}, cap=1)
    scheduler = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=3)])], seed=1,
                          counting=table)
    for outcome in (Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)

    assert (scheduler.shortfalls, scheduler.counts("a").in_a_row) == ([], 1)
    scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)
    assert [(s.because, s.trials) for s in scheduler.shortfalls] == [(CAP, 3)]


def test_the_tasks_repeat_limit_holds_in_a_block_counted_by_its_own_table_unless_it_sets_one():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "No: the task's limit applies unless the block sets its own": a block
    counting by a named table, which sets no limit, keeps the structure's limit of 1; a block whose
    own table sets 2 is moved past at its third failure in a row instead. A named table removed the
    structure's limit."""
    def moved_past(table) -> list:
        block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=3)], counting=table)
        scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, cap=1))
        for _ in range(5):
            if scheduler.finished:
                break
            scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)
        return [short.after_trial for short in scheduler.shortfalls]

    assert moved_past(CORRECT_ONLY) == [1]
    assert moved_past(Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, cap=2)) == [2]


def test_a_rig_failure_anywhere_in_the_row_names_it_for_the_rig_and_an_ended_trial_forgets_it():
    """Task 7's review, Important 2: a shortfall is flagged as possible avoidance only when every
    failed try in the row was the animal's, so a lost tracker on the row's first try names it for
    the rig as surely as one on its last; and an answer, ending the trial, clears the rig's
    failures with the rest of its count, so the animal's own failures after it are flagged."""
    first = _breaking_on(cap=1, breaks=2, outcomes=(Outcome.TRACKER_LOST, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK))
    ended = _breaking_on(cap=1, breaks=4, target=5, outcomes=(
        Outcome.TRACKER_LOST, Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK))

    assert [(s.because, s.possible_avoidance) for s in first.shortfalls] == [(RIG, False)]
    assert [(s.because, s.possible_avoidance) for s in ended.shortfalls] == [(CAP, True)]


def test_a_drummed_answer_ends_its_trials_failed_tries_too():
    """The controller's ruling I3 on the plan's revision review: the PI's decision 7 ("1: an answer
    ends the trial") holds for an answer a drum brought back. `x` breaks and its repeat waits;
    drumming is switched on, and the repeat is answered wrong and drummed; drumming is switched
    off, which drops the drum; `x`'s next trial breaks once -- failure 1 of a new trial, so a limit
    of 1 places a repeat and forgives nothing. Reset after the drum's branch, which returns early,
    the wrong answer ended nothing, and the break forgave `x` as possible avoidance."""
    scheduler = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("x", {}, target=3),
                                                       Condition("y", {}, target=40)])],
                          seed=1, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, cap=1))

    def draw_x() -> None:
        for _ in range(10):
            if scheduler.next_trial().name == "x":
                return
            scheduler.record("y", Outcome.CORRECT)
        raise AssertionError("the seed draws x within ten trials")

    draw_x()
    scheduler.record("x", Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)
    scheduler.drum("block")
    draw_x()
    assert scheduler.drawn.repeat_of is not None, "x's next trial is its waiting repeat"
    scheduler.record("x", Outcome.WRONG_TARGET)
    scheduler.drum("off")
    draw_x()
    scheduler.record("x", Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)

    assert (scheduler.shortfalls, scheduler.counts("x").in_a_row) == ([], 1)
```

### Task 12: A rule judges every answer and only answers; a streak every try in order, a percentage by the training setting

The PI's decisions 1 to 4 of 2026-10-18 (asked one at a time, his words quoted; the ledger's lines "PI (2026-10-18), counting Q1" to "Q8" and "Q14"), and his decision 14:

1. **"2: only answers make or break a streak"** -- a rule judges answers only: right (a correct answer, a correct rejection) or wrong (a wrong target, a false alarm, an early or a late response or error, and a miss where the task makes no response an answer). A trial with no answer -- a fixation break, no fixation, a lost tracker, a fault, a hang -- neither adds to nor breaks a streak or a percentage. Built for the default tables; **not** where a block's table gave a non-answer a paying class (a training block counting a fixation break as an error was judged on it), nor where a block named its own judged outcomes (`Block.criterion_over`).
2. **"Yes, every answer counts in the 20"** -- every answer is judged, whatever the block's table says about paying, "until N correct" included (settles XC-367: a wrong answer there paid nothing and was judged by nothing, so "80% over 20" was met by any 20 right answers).
3. **"Every answer counts in order"** -- "First tries and drummed tries alike: the wrong answer breaks the streak (back to 0), and the drummed correct starts it again at 1. A streak can only be built from correct answers in a row, whichever try they came on."
4. **A percentage rule under drumming**: "Perhaps a toggle in training?" -- read back and confirmed as a training setting for how drummed answers count toward a percentage, whose default he chose on 2026-10-18: **"Every answer"** -- "drummed answers go into the percentage in order by default, as for a streak; 'first answers only' is the setting switched to on purpose". With the setting on, his answers of 2026-10-12 ("Leave drummed repeats out") and of 2026-10-18 ("Judge its first answer") hold as built: only each trial's first answer is judged, and a trial whose first try had no answer is judged by its first answer.

And decision 14, **"Both wrong answers (as built)"**: an early and a late response are wrong answers -- each pays the trial, counts against a percentage, breaks a streak and is drummed. Nothing changes in the code; `task.Family`'s docstring, which said where they fall was open since 2026-09-26, now says what he decided.

**How a streak is told from a percentage** (Appendix B, item 14): a block's rule is `criterion = (proportion, window)`, as built; **a proportion of 1.0 is a streak**, "N correct in a row" -- every one of the last N answers right -- and any lower proportion a percentage. The two decisions differ only under drumming with the setting on, so the setting reads the proportion and nothing else.

**`Block.criterion_over` is removed** (Appendix B, item 15). It let a block name which outcomes its rule judges -- a narrower set, or a non-answer -- and both now contradict the PI's two rules ("answers only", "every answer"). Nothing outside a test set it (C2b's plans would have); its reason, that judging engagement makes a criterion track it, is the "answers only" rule itself.

**A hold-training block's scored break: the PI's decision 17 of 2026-10-18** (the revision's engineering review, I2; asked: 'Take a block that trains a hold, e.g. "hold fixation; done at 80% held over the last 10". Under your rule that only answers count, a broken hold is left out, so the rule is met by any 10 holds, even if the animal broke 9 times in 10. Should a broken hold count as a miss in such a block?'): **"Yes, where the block says so"** -- "Only where a block itself declares a broken hold as a scored trial (a hold-training block) does it count as a miss toward the percentage. Everywhere else, breaks stay out, as you decided." So an outcome that **the block's own table** (`Block.counting`) gives `COUNTED`, though it is no answer, is judged too -- wrong, never right (`Scheduler._scored`); a structure's table, and the named tables, keep decision 1 exactly. `Block.counting`'s comment, whose training-block example this makes true, says so.

**What is an answer is the structure's** (the controller's ruling M8 on the review): `_class` read a block table's own `withholding`, so a block's table could make no response a miss over a structure that does not withhold; it no longer does, as `_answer` never did.

**`Scheduler.streak`**, whether the open block's rule is a streak, is for the page (Task 9: the controller's ruling M4).

The setting is `Scheduler.first_answers`, off by default; Task 14 lets a trainer switch it from the console, recorded with who, beside the training aids.

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/scheduler.py` (imports; `Block.criterion`'s and `Block.counting`'s comments; `Block.criterion_over` removed; `Scheduler.first_answers`; `_answered`'s comment; `_answer`, `_scored`, `_first_answers` and `streak`, before `_class`; `_class`; `record`'s docstring and its judging)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/task.py` (`Family`'s docstring)
- Modify (test): `tests/test_scheduler.py` (one test replaced, two changed, five new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `task.ANSWERS`, `RIGHT` (Task 1); `Scheduler._answered`, `drawn` (Tasks 4, 6).
- Produces: `Scheduler(..., first_answers: bool = False)`; `Scheduler._answer(outcome) -> bool`; `Scheduler._scored(outcome) -> bool`; `Scheduler._first_answers -> bool`; `Scheduler.streak -> bool`; `Block` without `criterion_over`; `_class` reading the structure's `withholding` only.

```python
def test_the_criterion_window_can_use_a_different_rule_from_the_counter():
    """They are different questions. A block may count every presentation toward
    its quota while judging performance only on completed choices."""
    block = Block(
        name="main",
        conditions=[Condition("near", {}, target=100)],
        counting=PRESENTED,
        criterion=(0.8, 2),
        criterion_over=frozenset({Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET}),
    )
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)

    for outcome in (Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.NO_RESPONSE, Outcome.CORRECT):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)

    assert scheduler.finished, "two correct choices; the no-response was not judged"
```

```python
def test_a_rule_judges_answers_only():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18: a rule judges answers only -- right, or wrong, a miss among them where
    the task makes no response an answer -- and "a trial with no answer neither adds to nor breaks a
    streak or a percentage": a fixation break, and no response where the structure does not
    withhold, are no answers, so the two right answers around them are in a row. Where it
    withholds, no response is a miss, judged."""
    def window(withholding: bool) -> list:
        block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=100)], criterion=(1.0, 2))
        scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1,
                              counting=Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT, withholding=withholding))
        for outcome in (Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK, Outcome.NO_RESPONSE,
                        Outcome.CORRECT):
            scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)
        return list(scheduler._window)

    assert window(withholding=False) == [1, 1]
    assert window(withholding=True) == [0, 1]
```

```python
def test_every_answer_is_judged_under_until_n_correct_too():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "Yes, every answer counts in the 20" (settling XC-367): a wrong answer

def test_a_streak_under_drumming_judges_every_answer_in_order(first_answers):
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "Every answer counts in order": "the wrong answer breaks the streak (back
    to 0), and the drummed correct starts it again at 1. A streak can only be built from correct
    answers in a row, whichever try they came on." A streak is a rule of proportion 1.0, "N correct
    in a row", whatever the training setting says of a percentage."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=100)], criterion=(1.0, 3),

@pytest.mark.parametrize(("first_answers", "judged"), [(False, [0, 1, 0, 1]), (True, [0, 0])])
def test_a_percentage_under_drumming_judges_every_answer_unless_set_to_first_answers(
        first_answers, judged):
    """The PI, 2026-10-18: by default a percentage rule counts every answer in order, drummed tries
    included ("Every answer"); the training setting "first answers only" judges each trial's first
    answer, and a trial whose first try had no answer is judged by its first answer ("Judge its
    first answer")."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=100)], criterion=(0.5, 10),

def test_a_hold_training_block_judges_a_broken_hold_it_scores_as_a_miss():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "Yes, where the block says so": "Only where a block itself declares a
    broken hold as a scored trial (a hold-training block) does it count as a miss toward the
    percentage. Everywhere else, breaks stay out, as you decided." Judged on answers alone, "80%
    held over the last 10" was met by any 10 holds with the animal breaking 9 times in 10 (the
    revision's engineering review, I2). The same breaks under the structure's table stay out."""
    def drill(block_table) -> Scheduler:
        block = Block(name="hold", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=1000)],
                      criterion=(0.8, 10), counting=block_table)
        return Scheduler([block], seed=1, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED))

    scored = drill(Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, outcomes={"fixation_break": COUNTED}))
    plain = drill(None)
    for n in range(100):
        outcome = Outcome.CORRECT if n % 10 == 0 else Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK
        for scheduler in (scored, plain):
            scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)

    assert list(scored._window) == [1] + [0] * 9 and not scored.finished
    assert list(plain._window) == [1] * 10


def test_a_blocks_own_table_cannot_make_no_response_an_answer():
    """The controller's ruling M8 on the plan's revision review: what is an answer is the
    structure's, as `_answer` reads it. A block's table saying it withholds, over a structure that
    does not, made no response a miss, counted; it is classed as that table classes any other
    break after the stimulus, and no rule judges it."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=3)], criterion=(1.0, 2),
                  counting=Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT, withholding=True))
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED))

    assert scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.NO_RESPONSE) == SPENT
```

```python
    def _answer(self, outcome: Outcome | None) -> bool:
        """Whether `outcome` is an answer, right or wrong (the PI, 2026-10-18): one of
        `task.ANSWERS` -- a correct answer, a correct rejection, a wrong target, a false alarm, an
        early or a late response or error -- or no response where the structure makes withholding
        an answer, a miss (`Counting.withholding`, the structure's, as `_class` reads it). A trial
        with no answer -- a break, no fixation, a lost tracker, a fault, a hang -- is none."""
        if outcome in ANSWERS:
            return True
        return (outcome is Outcome.NO_RESPONSE and self.counting is not None
                and self.counting.withholding)

    def _scored(self, outcome: Outcome | None) -> bool:
        """Whether the open block's own table scores `outcome` though it is no answer: a block
        training a hold that counts a broken hold as an error that pays (`COUNTED`). Its rule judges
        it wrong, never right (the PI, 2026-10-18, "Yes, where the block says so": "Only where a
        block itself declares a broken hold as a scored trial (a hold-training block) does it count
        as a miss toward the percentage. Everywhere else, breaks stay out, as you decided.")."""
        own = self.block.counting
        return (outcome is not None and own is not None
                and own.outcomes.get(outcome.value) == COUNTED)

    @property
    def _first_answers(self) -> bool:
        """Whether a rule judges only each trial's first answer: the training setting
        `first_answers` is on and the block's rule is a percentage. **A rule of proportion 1.0 is a
        streak**, "N correct in a row", which judges every answer in order whatever the setting
        (the PI, 2026-10-18: "Every answer counts in order")."""
        criterion = self.block.criterion
        return self.first_answers and criterion is not None and criterion[0] < 1.0

    @property
    def streak(self) -> bool:
        """Whether the open block's rule is a streak, "N correct in a row": a criterion of
        proportion 1.0, which the training setting `first_answers` leaves judging every answer."""
        criterion = self.block.criterion
        return criterion is not None and criterion[0] >= 1.0
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Prove they can fail**

- `_answer`'s `if outcome in ANSWERS:` → `if False:` (no answer judged): `test_a_rule_judges_answers_only_whatever_the_block_counts` fails.
- `_answer`'s last line → `return False` (a miss no answer): the same test fails (its withholding case).
- `record`'s `if answer and not (self._answered and self._first_answers):` → `if answer and not self._answered:` (first answers only, always, as built): `test_a_streak_under_drumming_judges_every_answer_in_order[False]` fails, and `test_a_percentage_under_drumming_judges_every_answer_unless_set_to_first_answers[False-judged0]`.
- `record`'s `judged = self._answer(outcome) or self._scored(outcome)` → `judged = kind == COUNTED` (judged by paying, as built): `test_every_answer_is_judged_under_until_n_correct_too` fails; → `judged = self._answer(outcome)` (a scored break left out): `test_a_hold_training_block_judges_a_broken_hold_it_scores_as_a_miss` fails.
- `_scored`'s `and own.outcomes.get(outcome.value) == COUNTED)` → `and False)`: the same test fails.
- `_class`'s `table = replace(self.block.counting, withholding=False)` → `pass` (a block's own `withholding` read): `test_a_blocks_own_table_cannot_make_no_response_an_answer` fails.
- `_first_answers`'s `and criterion[0] < 1.0` dropped (a streak under the setting judged on first answers): `test_a_streak_under_drumming_judges_every_answer_in_order[True]` fails.
- `_first_answers`'s `return self.first_answers and` → `return False and` (the setting ignored): `test_a_percentage_under_drumming_judges_every_answer_unless_set_to_first_answers[True-judged1]` fails.
- `record`'s `self._answered = self._answered or judged` → `self._answered = judged` (a break after a wrong answer letting the next answer in): `test_a_drummed_trials_first_answer_is_judged_when_its_first_try_ended_before_one` fails.

Body: the PI's decisions 1 to 4, 14 and 17 of 2026-10-18, quoted, and the answers of 2026-10-12 and 2026-10-18 they keep inside the setting; XC-367 settled; a hold-training block's scored break judged a miss; the structure deciding what is an answer (ruling M8); a streak told by its proportion (Appendix B, item 14); `criterion_over` removed (Appendix B, item 15); the red's TypeErrors; the count; the breaks seen failing.

---

### Task 13: A criterion block's targets are a minimum: it ends once both hold, and draws every condition past its targets

The PI's decision 6 of 2026-10-18 (asked about XC-364: a criterion block's targets), **"Both: criterion and every target"** -- "The block ends only once the criterion is met and every target is used up; conditions keep coming until both hold." Built: a criterion block ended on its criterion alone, its targets a cap on what could be drawn, so a block whose targets were all paid before its criterion was met had nothing left to draw and the run faulted (`IndexError`, XC-364, which Task 11 filed for C2b and now closes).

**What is drawn past the targets** (Appendix B, item 16): once every target of a criterion block is used up and its criterion is not met, **every condition of the block is drawn again, by the block's own order** -- a shuffled block one of each per pass, a block drawing with replacement by its weights, a constrained one within its runs -- until the criterion is met. Nothing is owed then, so a break past the targets is not shown again and no repeat limit is reached -- its row's `counting` still says `owed`, which past the targets means "no answer, not shown again" (the controller's ruling M2: not reclassed) -- and **drumming goes on** (a drummed trial still comes back until it is right, and the block waits for it: decision 18). A condition moved past by its limit or a person's *clear* is drawn again too: past the targets the block draws what it has, and "keep coming" names no exception.

**A drum at the block's end: the PI's decision 18 of 2026-10-18** (the revision's engineering review, M11; asked: 'With drumming on, a block's percentage can be reached on a wrong answer, e.g. 4 right in a row then a wrong one still makes 80% of the last 5. Should the block end right there, or first bring that trial back until it is right?'): **"Finish the drum first"** -- "The block ends only after the drummed trial is answered right. If the extra tries drop the percentage below the bar, the block carries on. Training ends on a success." So **a block is finished only with no drum waiting** (`finished`). Two guards change what they guard: `advance`'s `self._drum = None` is now reached only by an advance asked of a block that is not finished, which the run's loop never does -- it stays, the guard Task 6's fix round proved, and its test now leaves a block on purpose; and a drumming switch's carry to the next block (`_carried`, `self.finished and not self.done`) is asked **before** *off* drops a waiting drum, since dropping one that holds a met block open would meet the end rule and call the switch the next block's, switching off a next block's own drumming. Its reason stands -- a switch at a boundary where a block's end rule is met is the next block's -- and its test (Task 8's) still holds.

**What the record says**: each trial's row as any other -- its outcome, and its class as its table gives it (`counted`, `spent`, `owed`, `unpaid`) -- and the run's end row's per-condition counts (Task 15) show how many trials each condition took past its target. **What it costs** (Appendix B, item 17): a criterion never met no longer faults the run; it runs until the out-of-cage limit, a scheduled stop or a person stops it, as a block a person clears nothing in does today. A block time limit, which would end one sooner, is C2b's.

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/scheduler.py` (`Block.criterion`'s comment; `_drawable`; `past_targets`, before `_runs_long`; `drum`; `finished`; `record`'s drum)
- Modify (test): `tests/test_scheduler.py` (five new; four changed, one of them rewritten), `tests/test_taskd.py` (one rewritten)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Scheduler.owed`, `_window`, `drumming` (Tasks 3, 6, 12).
- Produces: `Scheduler.past_targets -> bool` (public: Task 9's page reads it); `finished` true only once a criterion block's criterion is met and every target is used up.

```python
def test_a_drummed_block_that_ends_on_a_wrong_first_try_opens_the_next_with_its_own_condition():
    """A criterion met on a wrong first try leaves a drum waiting; the next block must not draw it
    (its condition is not the next block's: `next_trial` raised `StopIteration`)."""
    blocks = [Block(name="A", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=100)], criterion=(0.5, 2),
                    drum=True),
              Block(name="B", conditions=[Condition("b", {}, target=2)])]
    scheduler = Scheduler(blocks, seed=1)
    for outcome in (Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)
    assert scheduler.finished and scheduler.drum_tries == 1, "a drum is waiting"
```

```python
def test_a_criterion_block_ends_only_once_its_criterion_is_met_and_every_target_is_used_up():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "Both: criterion and every target": "The block ends only once the
    criterion is met and every target is used up". Two right answers meet 100% over two, and the
    block, owing four trials, goes on until it owes none; its criterion alone ended it."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=3), Condition("b", {}, target=1)],
                  criterion=(1.0, 2))
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)
    for _ in range(2):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.CORRECT)

    assert list(scheduler._window) == [1, 1] and not scheduler.finished
    for _ in range(2):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.CORRECT)
    assert scheduler.finished and [scheduler.owed(n) for n in "ab"] == [0, 0]


def test_past_its_targets_a_criterion_block_draws_every_condition_until_the_criterion_is_met():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18 (settling XC-364): "conditions keep coming until both hold". Every target
    used up with the criterion unmet, the block draws all its conditions again, by its order, each
    answer counted as ever and nothing owed -- where it had nothing left to draw and the run
    faulted. A block without a criterion still draws nothing once its targets are used up."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition(n, {}, target=1) for n in "ab"],
                  criterion=(1.0, 3))
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)
    kinds, drawn = [], []
    for outcome in (Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET, Outcome.CORRECT,
                    Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.CORRECT):
        assert not scheduler.finished
        drawn.append(scheduler.next_trial().name)
        kinds.append(scheduler.record(drawn[-1], outcome))

    assert scheduler.finished and set(drawn[2:]) == {"a", "b"}
    assert kinds == [COUNTED] * 6 and [scheduler.owed(n) for n in "ab"] == [0, 0]
    fixed = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition(n, {}, target=1) for n in "ab"])],
                      seed=1)
    for _ in range(2):
        fixed.record(fixed.next_trial().name, Outcome.CORRECT)
    with pytest.raises(IndexError, match="no condition left to draw"):
        fixed.next_trial()


def test_a_drummed_criterion_block_goes_on_drumming_past_its_targets():
    """Drumming acted only while a condition owed, so past its targets a training block -- a streak,
    drummed -- stopped drumming: a wrong answer there comes back at once until it is right, as it
    did before the target was used up."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=1)], criterion=(1.0, 2),
                  drum=True)
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)
    drummed = []
    for outcome in (Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET, Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.CORRECT):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)
        drummed.append(scheduler.drawn.drummed)

    assert drummed == [False, False, True, False] and scheduler.finished


@pytest.mark.parametrize(("drummed", "finished"), [
    ((Outcome.CORRECT,), True),
    ((Outcome.WRONG_TARGET, Outcome.CORRECT), False),
])
def test_a_drummed_block_met_on_a_wrong_answer_finishes_its_drum_first(drummed, finished):
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "Finish the drum first": "The block ends only after the drummed trial is
    answered right. If the extra tries drop the percentage below the bar, the block carries on.
    Training ends on a success." Four right answers then a wrong one make 80% of the last 5, and
    the block waits for its drum: right at once, it ends there; wrong again, the percentage falls
    below the bar, and the block carries on once the drum is right."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=1)], criterion=(0.8, 5),
                  drum=True)
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)
    for outcome in (Outcome.CORRECT,) * 4 + (Outcome.WRONG_TARGET,):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)
    assert list(scheduler._window) == [1, 1, 1, 1, 0] and scheduler.drum_tries == 1
    assert not scheduler.finished, "met, but a drum is waiting"

    for outcome in drummed:
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)

    assert scheduler.drum_tries == 0 and scheduler.finished is finished


def test_stopping_a_drum_that_holds_a_met_block_open_is_this_blocks_switch():
    """*Stop drumming* while a drum holds open a block whose criterion is met ends the drum, and the
    block with it; the switch is this block's, so the next block's own drumming is not switched
    off. Asked after *off* had dropped the drum, the carry called it the next block's (reachable
    since the PI's "Finish the drum first", 2026-10-18)."""
    blocks = [Block(name="A", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=1)], criterion=(0.5, 2),
                    drum=True),
              Block(name="B", conditions=[Condition("b", {}, target=2)], drum=True)]
    scheduler = Scheduler(blocks, seed=1)
    for outcome in (Outcome.CORRECT, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET):
        scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, outcome)
    assert not scheduler.finished and scheduler.drum_tries == 1

    assert scheduler.drum("off") == "A" and scheduler.finished
    scheduler.advance()
    assert (scheduler.block.name, scheduler.drumming) == ("B", "block")
```

```python
    @property
    def finished(self) -> bool:
        """Whether the open block's end rule is met: every condition owes nothing, and where the
        block ends on a criterion, the criterion is met too -- **its targets are a minimum** (the
        PI, 2026-10-18, "Both: criterion and every target": "The block ends only once the criterion
        is met and every target is used up; conditions keep coming until both hold") -- **and no
        drum waits** ("Finish the drum first": "The block ends only after the drummed trial is
        answered right. If the extra tries drop the percentage below the bar, the block carries on.
        Training ends on a success.")."""
        if self._drum is not None:
            return False
        if self.block.criterion is not None:
            proportion, window = self.block.criterion
            if len(self._window) < window or sum(self._window) / len(self._window) < proportion:
                return False
        return all(self.owed(c.name) == 0 for c in self.block.conditions)
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Prove they can fail**

- `finished` returning `True` once the criterion is met (a line `return True` added inside its `if`, after `return False`: the criterion alone ending the block, as built): `test_a_criterion_block_ends_only_once_its_criterion_is_met_and_every_target_is_used_up` fails.
- `_drawable`'s `(self.owed(condition) > 0 or self.past_targets)` → `self.owed(condition) > 0`: `test_past_its_targets_a_criterion_block_draws_every_condition_until_the_criterion_is_met` fails (XC-364's `IndexError`).
- `past_targets`'s `self.block.criterion is not None and all(` → `all(` (a block with no criterion drawing past its targets): the same test fails (no `IndexError` from the fixed block).
- `record`'s `and (self.owed(condition) > 0 or self.past_targets)):` → `and self.owed(condition) > 0):`: `test_a_drummed_criterion_block_goes_on_drumming_past_its_targets` fails.
- `finished` without `if self._drum is not None:` and its `return False` (the block ending on a drummed wrong answer, as built): both cases of `test_a_drummed_block_met_on_a_wrong_answer_finishes_its_drum_first` fail, and `test_a_block_left_with_a_drum_waiting_opens_the_next_with_its_own_condition`.
- `drum` asking `self._carried = self.finished and not self.done` after *off* drops the drum (the line moved back below `self._drum = None`, as built): `test_stopping_a_drum_that_holds_a_met_block_open_is_this_blocks_switch` fails.
- `finished` without its criterion check (the `if self.block.criterion is not None:` block taken out: targets alone ending a criterion block): `tests/test_taskd.py`'s `test_a_criterion_block_ends_once_its_criterion_and_its_target_both_hold` fails (the ruling M7).

---

### Task 14: Drumming and *clear* are training aids: *clear* off until a trainer switches it on, a switch for it and the percentage setting, and every row marks the aids on

The PI's decisions 10 to 12 of 2026-10-18:

- **"Drumming and 'clear'"** are the training aids (asked which built features are): "clearing trials from the count is a training aid too: off by default, with the standing warning while it is available". And of drumming (asked how drummed answers count toward a percentage): "Drumming should only be used in training sessions, so perhaps in the training section, there is a toggle for how they are counted. By default training aids should be off, unless we have training default profiles. And there can be a warning that training aids are on in the console." No profile exists yet (XC-379, Task 11), so both are off as every run starts.
- **"Allow it, with a warning"** (asked what a block's drumming, or a trainer's, does in a piloting or recording session): "it works, and the console shows a standing warning that a training aid is on in a non-training session. The record says so on every trial."
- **"Trainer turns 'clear' on when needed"** (calibration, whose "No limit" of 2026-10-12 relied on *clear*): no exception -- if a target sticks, the trainer switches *clear* on, with the standing warning, and moves past it.

Built here, in the engine: **drumming** is already off until a block declares it or a trainer switches it (Tasks 6, 8), so it needs nothing but its mark; **_clear_** becomes off by default (`Scheduler.clearing`), and `Scheduler.clear` refuses with a sentence while it is off -- a control not switched on, never a refusal of a task; **a switch** (`link.Training`, kind `training`) turns *clear* or the percentage setting of Task 12 (`first_answers`) on or off for the rest of the run, recorded in `controls.jsonl` with who; and **every trial's row marks the aids on as it was drawn** (`aids`: `"drumming"`, `"clear"`). The console's standing warning, worded differently outside a training session, its switches and *clear* offered only while it is on, are Task 9's (the page). Nothing is refused by a session's kind.

**Each switch holds for the run it is made in** (Appendix B, item 18): a run starts with both aids off and the setting "every answer", as every run starts with drumming off, until per-animal training profiles exist (XC-379). **The switch reaches the session through a third branch in `Session._command`** (Appendix A, item 4: a new item for the PI's approval), beside the pause and the two he approved for Task 8, outside the function's welfare-listed parts; Task 11's byte-for-byte comparison takes all three out.

The drum's warning after ten failed tries named *clear one* as a way past the trial; with *clear* off by default it says the aid must be switched on first, and **where the drummed condition owes nothing** -- past a criterion block's targets, where *clear* is refused -- it names only *stop drumming* (the controller's ruling M3 on the revision's review; `taskd._drum_warned`). **A switch to the value already set is refused with its sentence**, as a pause of a paused session is, and records no control row (the controller's smaller ruling). `task.Counting.cap`'s docstring says what now ends its count and that *clear* is a training aid (the controller's ruling M1).

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/scheduler.py` (`TRAINING`; `Draw.aids`; `Scheduler.first_answers`' comment, `clearing`; `aids` and `train`, before `clear`; `clear`; `next_trial`)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/link.py` (docstrings; imports; `Training`; `Command`; `_encode_command`; `_command_from`)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/taskd.py` (`_drum_warned`, before `_appeared`; `Session._command`'s third branch; `Session._train`, before `_ends`; the trial row's `aids`; the drum's warning)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/task.py` (`Counting`'s docstring, its `cap`)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/record.py` (`SessionRecord.trial`'s `aids`)
- Modify (test): `tests/test_scheduler.py` (imports; two new; six switch *clear* on), `tests/test_link.py` (imports; two new), `tests/test_taskd.py` (imports; three new; three switch *clear* on; the drum's warning)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Scheduler.drumming`, `clear`, `first_answers` (Tasks 6, 8, 12); `Session._control`, `_refuse` (unchanged).
- Produces: `scheduler.TRAINING = ("clear", "first_answers")`; `Scheduler(..., clearing: bool = False)`; `Scheduler.aids -> tuple[str, ...]`; `Scheduler.train(setting: str, on: bool) -> None`; `Draw.aids`; `link.Training(setting: str, on: bool, by: Actor)`, `KIND = "training"`, wire `{"kind": "training", "by", "setting", "on"}`; `Session._train(command, index)`, refusing a switch to the value already set; `taskd._drum_warned(condition, index, owed) -> str`; `controls.jsonl` rows of kind `training` (`setting`, `was`, `now`); each trial row's `aids`, a list.

```python
def test_clear_is_a_training_aid_off_until_a_trainer_switches_it_on():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "Drumming and 'clear'": clearing trials from the count is a training
    aid, off by default; a clear while it is off is refused with a sentence and changes nothing --
    a control not switched on, not a refusal of a task. The trainer switches it on, and off, for the
    rest of the run, and the percentage setting beside it; nothing else is switched so."""
    scheduler = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=3)])], seed=1)
    off = ("clear is a training aid, and it is off in this run; a trainer switches it on to clear "
           "a trial from the count")

    assert scheduler.clear("a") == off and scheduler.shortfalls == []
    scheduler.train("clear", True)
    assert scheduler.clear("a") is None and scheduler.owed("a") == 2
    scheduler.train("clear", False)
    scheduler.train("first_answers", True)
    assert scheduler.clear("a") == off and scheduler.first_answers
    assert TRAINING == ("clear", "first_answers")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="a training setting is clear or first_answers"):
        scheduler.train("drum", True)


def test_each_draw_names_the_training_aids_on_as_it_was_drawn():
    """The PI, 2026-10-18: while a training aid is on "the record marks it on every trial" --
    drumming, by a block's declaration or a trainer's switch, and *clear*. The percentage setting is
    no aid; its switch is in the control rows."""
    scheduler = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=5)])], seed=1)
    seen = []
    for switch in (None, ("clear", True), ("drum", "run"), ("first_answers", True)):
        if switch is not None and switch[0] == "drum":
            scheduler.drum(switch[1])
        elif switch is not None:
            scheduler.train(*switch)
        seen.append(scheduler.next_trial() and scheduler.drawn.aids)
        scheduler.record("a", Outcome.CORRECT)

    assert seen == [(), ("clear",), ("drumming", "clear"), ("drumming", "clear")]
```

```python
                                     Training(setting="first_answers", on=False, by=Box("jake"))])
def test_a_training_switch_survives_the_wire_with_who_sent_it(command):
    """The PI, 2026-10-18: the training aid *clear* and the percentage setting, each switched on and
    off by a trainer, recorded with who."""

    assert Training.KIND == "training"


@pytest.mark.parametrize(

    [
        ({"kind": "training", "setting": "drum", "on": True, "by": JAKE},
         "a training setting is clear or first_answers"),
        ({"kind": "training", "on": True, "by": JAKE},
         "a training setting is clear or first_answers"),
        ({"kind": "training", "setting": "clear", "on": "yes", "by": JAKE}, "on is true or false"),
    ],
)
def test_a_malformed_training_switch_is_refused_where_it_is_decoded(fields, said):
    with pytest.raises(CommandRefused) as refused:
        _decode_command(_packed(**fields))

    assert refused.value.name == "training" and said in refused.value.why
```

```python
def test_clear_is_refused_until_a_trainer_switches_it_on_and_each_row_marks_the_aids_on(tmp_path):
    """The PI, 2026-10-18: *clear* is a training aid, off by default; refused with the sentence
    until a trainer switches it on, which `controls.jsonl` records with who; and every trial's row
    marks the aids on as it was drawn. A second switch to the same value is refused with its
    sentence, as a pause of a paused session is, and records nothing."""
    link = _Drained({1: [ClearOwed(condition="near", by=Box("jake"))],
                     2: [Training(setting="clear", on=True, by=Box("jake")),
                         Training(setting="clear", on=True, by=Box("jake"))],
                     3: [ClearOwed(condition="near", by=Box("jake"))]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()]), link=link)

    session.run()

    assert [(name, why) for name, _, why in session.refusals] == [
        ("clear", "clear is a training aid, and it is off in this run; a trainer switches it on "
                  "to clear a trial from the count"),
        ("training", "clear is already on, so nothing changed")]
    (switched,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "training"]
    assert (switched["setting"], switched["was"], switched["now"], switched["by"],
            switched["trial_index"]) == ("clear", False, True, {"kind": "box", "name": "jake"}, 1)
    assert [row["kind"] for row in _controls_rows(session)] == ["training", "clear"]
    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    assert rows[0]["aids"] == [] and {tuple(row["aids"]) for row in rows[1:]} == {("clear",)}


def test_a_blocks_drumming_works_in_a_piloting_session_and_its_rows_mark_it(tmp_path):
    """The PI, 2026-10-18, "Allow it, with a warning": a training aid is meant for training
    sessions, and in a piloting or recording one "it works, and the console shows a standing warning
    ... The record says so on every trial." Nothing refuses it."""
    block = Block(name="drill", conditions=[Condition("c", {}, target=10)], drum=True)
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[block], session_kind="piloting"))

    session.run()

    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    assert {tuple(row["aids"]) for row in rows} == {("drumming",)}
    assert any(row["drummed"] for row in rows) and session.stop_kind == "completed"


def test_the_drum_warning_names_only_stop_drumming_where_the_condition_owes_nothing():
    """The controller's ruling M3 on the plan's revision review: past a criterion block's targets
    the drummed condition owes nothing, so *clear one* would be refused there; the warning after
    ten failed tries names only *stop drumming*. Where it owes, *clear one* once switched on."""
    owing = ("c has failed 10 tries in a row under drumming, the last trial 9; drumming goes on: "
             "clear one moves past it once clear is switched on, stop drumming ends it")

    assert _drum_warned("c", 9, 1) == owing
    assert _drum_warned("c", 9, 0) == (
        "c has failed 10 tries in a row under drumming, the last trial 9; drumming goes on: stop "
        "drumming ends it")
```

```python
    @property
    def aids(self) -> tuple:
        """The training aids on now (the PI, 2026-10-18, "Drumming and 'clear'"): `"drumming"`
        while trials are drummed, by a block's declaration or a trainer's switch, and `"clear"`
        while a person may clear a trial from the count. Each trial's row marks them (`Draw.aids`),
        and the console shows a standing warning while any is on."""
        return tuple(name for name, on in (("drumming", self.drumming is not None),
                                           ("clear", self.clearing)) if on)

    def train(self, setting: str, on: bool) -> None:
        """Switch a training setting on or off for the rest of the run, as a trainer does from the
        console (the PI, 2026-10-18; `TRAINING`): `"clear"`, the training aid that lets a person
        clear a trial from the count, or `"first_answers"`, which judges only each drummed trial's
        first answer for a percentage rule. Both are off as a run starts."""
        if setting not in TRAINING:
            raise ValueError(f"a training setting is {' or '.join(TRAINING)}, not {setting!r}")
        if setting == "clear":
            self.clearing = on
        else:
            self.first_answers = on
```

```python
    def _train(self, command, index: int) -> None:
        """**Switch a training setting** (`link.Training`; the PI, 2026-10-18): the training aid
        *clear*, off by default, or the setting "first answers only" for a percentage rule under
        drumming, on or off for the rest of the run (`Scheduler.train`); `controls.jsonl` records
        it with who switched it. In every kind of session ("Allow it, with a warning": a training
        aid is meant for training sessions and works in the others, the console warning while one
        is on and every trial's row marking it). **A switch to the value already set is refused**,
        with its sentence, as a pause of a paused session is."""
        scheduler = self._scheduler
        was = scheduler.clearing if command.setting == "clear" else scheduler.first_answers
        if was == command.on:
            # As a pause of a paused session is: nothing changes, so nothing is recorded.
            state = "on" if command.on else "off"
            self._refuse("training", command.by,
                         f"{command.setting} is already {state}, so nothing changed")
            return
        scheduler.train(command.setting, command.on)
        self._control(
            "training", command.by,
            f"{command.setting} {'on' if command.on else 'off'} for the rest of the run",
            index, setting=command.setting, was=was, now=command.on,
        )
```
