`docs/references/decisions.md`: insert

```markdown
### TSK-52 — Drumming: a training aid where a trial that is not right comes straight back until it is, declared by a block or switched on for a block or a run by the trainer; a streak judges every try, a percentage every try unless set to first answers only; a console warning after ten failed tries in a row; recorded
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI ("a training tool ... where a trial is repeated until correct": its own option, the same trial back immediately until correct, switched on per block or run by the trainer, recorded as a change); 2026-10-12, the PI ("Leave drummed repeats out": "Only each trial's first try is judged for the criterion. The repeats are still recorded as what they were."; "After 10 in a row": "A warning on the console after 10 failed tries in a row", drumming going on). That a correct rejection ends a drum is the controller's ruling on the plan's engineering review (I1), engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#16-counting-and-repeats-n13-nr1-nr2) and its note of YYYY-MM-DD; [`wl_xcon/scheduler.py`](../../wl_xcon/scheduler.py) (`Block.drum`, `Scheduler.drum`, `DRUM_WARNING`); [the engine C2a plan](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-engine-c2a.md#the-pis-decisions-it-builds-binding).
- **Basis:** PI decision. An established option: NIMH MonkeyLogic's "repeat immediately" plays "the same condition ... repeatedly until the trial succeeds" [@nimhmonkeylogic2026task] ("On error"), the alternative TSK-30 records the PI rejecting as the default; here it is a trainer's tool, never the default. Leaving the repeats out of the criterion is the PI's; the plan's science review found that judging them let an animal that always picks one side meet a 60% criterion at 50% true accuracy (its D2, a probe).
- **Revised:** 2026-10-18, the PI: under drumming **a streak judges every answer in order**, drummed tries included ("Every answer counts in order": "the wrong answer breaks the streak (back to 0), and the drummed correct starts it again at 1"), and **so does a percentage by default** ("Every answer"); "first answers only" is a training setting a trainer switches on, within which his answers of 2026-10-12 ("Leave drummed repeats out") and 2026-10-18 ("Judge its first answer": "If the first try ends before any answer, the first try that does answer is judged. Repeats after a wrong answer stay out, as you chose.") hold. **Drumming is a training aid** ("Drumming and 'clear'"): off unless a block declares it or a trainer switches it on, until per-animal training profiles exist (XC-379); in a piloting or recording session it works, with a standing warning on the console ("Allow it, with a warning"); every trial's row marks it. And answering the plan's revision review: **a block's end waits for its drum** ("Finish the drum first": "The block ends only after the drummed trial is answered right. If the extra tries drop the percentage below the bar, the block carries on. Training ends on a success."). Built YYYY-MM-DD by engine build C2a; the setting's reach (percentages only, a streak being a rule of proportion 1.0) is engineering, the plan's Appendix B item 14, which he approved.

### TSK-53 — The reference tasks' counting tables, as the PI confirmed them: each naming the state its stimulus appears in; no response a miss in both detection tasks, and leaving the target before its hold a target break; a break after the stimulus owed in the detection tasks, the search training variant and the calibration task, and spent in the search task; no repeat limit in any
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI (no response a miss in both detection tasks); 2026-10-12, the PI (no limit for calibration: "As drafted. The trainer moves past a target with the console's 'clear' button."); 2026-10-13, the PI, answering the engine C2a plan's Appendix B: items 1 and 2, "Approve"; item 3, "Task names it, required" (TSK-29); items 5 to 13, "Accept, target break" -- "Take the plan's tables, make leaving the target early a target break in the two detection tasks, and give visual_search_training no repeat limit." The question said these tables are engine test material, run only in the simulator, and that real tasks' tables are set with him in wl-xtasks.
- **Where:** [`tasks/`](../../tasks/) (each structure's `counting`); [the engine C2a plan's Appendix B](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-engine-c2a.md#appendix-b-for-the-pi-to-confirm); `tests/test_reference_tasks.py`'s `CONFIRMED_COUNTING`.
- **Basis:** PI decision. That a miss is an answer where withholding is one is signal detection theory, as TSK-27 records [@stanislaw1999calculation] (abstract). Spending a break in the pop-out search keeps its trial sequence as drawn: macaque pop-out performance depends on whether the previous trial's features and target position repeat [@bichot2002priming] (abstract), and priming of pop-out is the lab's own measure [@westerberg2020priming] (full text). Each other break rule is the PI's choice for the task, with the plan's reason beside it.
- **Revised:** 2026-10-18, the PI: calibration has no exception to *clear* being a training aid -- "Trainer turns 'clear' on when needed": if a target sticks, the trainer switches *clear* on, with the standing warning, and moves past it.

### TSK-54 — With no repeat limit set, a condition is repeated until done; a limit counts failed tries in a row of one trial, which any answer ends, and the task's limit holds in every block unless the block sets one; a person may clear one trial of a condition's count, or the rest, from the console, once the training aid *clear* is on, recorded with who did it
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI ("no limit (repeat until done)", and "there should be some way in the console to manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count"); 2026-10-12, the PI ("Tries in a row of one trial": "The condition is moved past after 3 failed repeats in a row, and the count resets once it is done."). *Clear the rest* is the controller's ruling on the plan's science review (D5), and naming a shortfall the rig made rather than flagging it as avoidance its ruling on the engineering review (M4), both engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#16-counting-and-repeats-n13-nr1-nr2) item 6 and its note of YYYY-MM-DD; [`wl_xcon/task.py`](../../wl_xcon/task.py) (`Counting.cap`); [`wl_xcon/scheduler.py`](../../wl_xcon/scheduler.py) (`Scheduler.clear`, `RIG`); [`wl_xcon/link.py`](../../wl_xcon/link.py) (`ClearOwed`).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published source.
- **Revised:** 2026-10-18, the PI: what the limit counts ("1: an answer ends the trial": any answer, right or wrong, starts the count over), whose limit ("No: the task's limit applies unless the block sets its own") and what *clear one* does ("End that waiting trial; count starts over"); and **_clear_ is a training aid** ("Drumming and 'clear'"): off by default, switched on and off by a trainer, recorded with who, a standing warning on the console while it is on, every trial's row marking it, and a clear sent while it is off refused with a sentence. Built YYYY-MM-DD by engine build C2a (`Scheduler._cap`, `clear`, `train`; `link.Training`).

### TSK-55 — A trial that reaches the longest trial, five minutes at the session's rate, is a missed acquisition: recorded as a hang and owed, repeated like a fault, and said on the console
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI ("it isn't a fixation break, it is a missed acquisition"; XC-287); the cap derived from 300 s at the session's rate is the engine spec §6.8's, 2026-10-07. The console's warning is the controller's ruling on the plan's science review (D4), engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3) item 8 and its note of YYYY-MM-DD; [`wl_xcon/run.py`](../../wl_xcon/run.py) (`LONGEST_TRIAL_S`); [`wl_xcon/task.py`](../../wl_xcon/task.py) (`HANG_COUNTS_AS`).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published source.

### TSK-56 — A "balanced" block-sequence order balances within each run
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI.
- **Where:** [the engine C2a plan](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-engine-c2a.md#the-pis-decisions-it-builds-binding), decision 7; built by engine build C2b with the block-sequence policies (engine spec §13.5).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published source for the run as the unit.

```

- [ ] **Step 6: The asks of the sibling repositories** (the engineering review's Minor 9), written where the house writes them:

`docs/pending-wl-preproc-amendments.md`: insert

```markdown
# OPEN — engine build C2a: each trial's count and each run's shortfalls

Opened YYYY-MM-DD by engine build C2a ([its plan](superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-engine-c2a.md), "Calls taken in this plan", calls 10 and 11), backlog XC-365. Read against wl-preproc's `wl_preproc/events/rigtrials.py` (`_FIELDS`) and `rigruns.py` through the worktree's `wl-preproc` link, 2026-10-11.

## What changed on our side

Each `trials.jsonl` line keeps the six fields wl-preproc requires and gains eight beside them: `counting` (`counted`, `spent`, `owed` or `unpaid`: what the trial did to its condition's count), `shown` (whether its stimulus had appeared), `repeat_of` (the `index` of the trial it shows again), `at_once` (whether it came straight after that one), `previous` (the condition before it in its block), `drummed` (a drum's repeat), `drumming` (`block`, `run` or null) and `aids` (the training aids on as it was drawn: `drumming`, `clear`). Each `runs.jsonl` **end** row gains `shortfalls`: a list of `{block, condition, trials, because, possible_avoidance, after_trial, by}`, `because` one of `cap`, `rig` or `cleared`; `conditions`: a list of each block's per-condition `{block, condition, trials, presentations, right, wrong, right_first, owed, repeats, moved_past, possible_avoidance, drummed, failing}`, a trial and its drummed repeats one trial (the PI, 2026-10-18); and `presentations`, its lines, while its `trials` now counts trials, a trial and its drummed repeats one -- before C2a the two were equal. A trial that faulted has no line, and is in neither. Past a criterion block's targets nothing is owed, so a trial line's `counting` of `owed` there means "no answer, not shown again". No new `runs.jsonl` row kind: the shortfalls ride the end row, since wl-preproc reports any row but a start or an end as a problem.

## The ask

**Read them into the published dataset**, so an analysis can leave owed trials out, tell a spent trial from an answer, follow the realized sequence, tell a trial run under a training aid, and see each condition's counts, its forgiven debt and whether it was flagged as possible avoidance. Both readers tolerate the fields today (a trial line's extra fields are taken as they are; an end row is read with `.get`), so nothing breaks before this is done.

## What is deliberately not asked for

No new escape, code or marker: nothing here is strobed. No change to the six required trial fields.

---

```

- [ ] **Step 7: The sweep.** As C1 did: **CI's per-push gate** sweeps every function of each module a push changes (`--changed-only --shard k/12`); after the last task, one CI run of the branch against `main` is read **shard by shard**, every line a real `N failed`, never the job's color, and a shard's log counts as read only if it ends with the gate's closing line and "Cleaning up orphan processes" (`gh run view --log` drops lines past a 64 KiB one; the per-job API, `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/jobs/<id>/logs`, has them all). The modules: `task`, `check`, `scheduler`, `run`, `taskd`, `record`, `link`, `serve`, `web`, `review` -- none new, so `tools/mutation_gate.py`'s lists stay as they are. Task 9 changes `tests/_frames.py`, which is in `mutation_gate.GLOBAL`, so that push's gate escalates to a full sweep anyway. **The full sweep too**: this branch changes `tasks/`, on which `--changed-only` never escalates, so one `mutation-full` run of the branch (`workflow_dispatch`) is read shard by shard the same way. Each function caught only by `timed out after 300s` or an import error (XC-275's verdicts) is then neutered locally (`tools/mutate.py`'s own `_neuter_source`) with its own test file run alone, and must fail a named test, against a control of the same files unmutated. A survivor gets a test in a fix commit before anything else.

- [ ] **Step 8: Welfare, byte for byte** -- each listed function's source at the branch's base and at its tip, and `Session._command` with its three new branches taken out (Task 8's two and Task 14's, Appendix A items 1 and 4):

```bash
cd <worktree> && BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD main) && $PY - "$BASE" <<'EOF'
import ast, subprocess, sys

BASE = sys.argv[1]
LISTED = {
    "wl_xcon/preflight.py": ["gate", "out_of_cage"],
    "wl_xcon/service.py": ["Service._open", "Service._end", "Service._unended",
                           "Service._close_stranded", "Service._start", "_unasked", "_folder_name"],
    "wl_xcon/taskd.py": ["OUTSIDE_A_RUN", "Session._ends", "Session._hold",
                         "Session._manual_reward", "Session.set", "Session._schedule"],
    "wl_xcon/link.py": ["_setting"],
    "wl_xcon/cli.py": ["_settle_departure", "_settle_return"],
}
#: The three branches engine build C2a adds to `Session._command`, outside its listed parts (its
#: `held` pass-through and its one `except` line): taken out, the rest must be the base's.
ADDED = '''        if isinstance(command, _link.ClearOwed):
            self._clear(command, index)
            return
        if isinstance(command, _link.Drum):
            self._drum(command, index)
            return
        if isinstance(command, _link.Training):
            self._train(command, index)
            return
'''


def named(nodes, name):
    for node in nodes:
        if getattr(node, "name", None) == name:
            return node
        if isinstance(node, ast.Assign) and any(getattr(t, "id", None) == name for t in node.targets):
            return node
    raise LookupError(name)


def source(text, dotted):
    node, nodes = None, ast.parse(text).body
    for part in dotted.split("."):
        node = named(nodes, part)
        nodes = getattr(node, "body", [])
    return ast.get_source_segment(text, node)


def base_text(path):
    return subprocess.run(["git", "show", f"{BASE}:{path}"], capture_output=True, text=True,
                          check=True).stdout


for path, names in LISTED.items():
    base, tip = base_text(path), open(path, encoding="utf-8").read()
    for name in names:
        print("same" if source(base, name) == source(tip, name) else "DIFFERS", path, name)
base, tip = base_text("wl_xcon/taskd.py"), open("wl_xcon/taskd.py", encoding="utf-8").read()
command = source(tip, "Session._command")
print("added once" if command.count(ADDED) == 1 else "ADDED NOT FOUND ONCE", "Session._command")
print("same" if command.replace(ADDED, "") == source(base, "Session._command") else "DIFFERS",
      "wl_xcon/taskd.py Session._command, the three added branches taken out")
EOF
git diff --stat "$BASE" -- wl_xcon/bounds.py wl_xcon/welfare.py wl_xcon/marks.py wl_xcon/stranded.py
git show "$BASE":wl_xcon/cli.py | grep -c '_marks.depart(session, departure)'; grep -c '_marks.depart(session, departure)' wl_xcon/cli.py
```

Expected: every line `same` and one `added once`; the `git diff --stat` prints nothing; both counts `1`. If anything differs, it goes to the PI in plain terms before the merge.

## Not in C2a

What the rest of build C2, and the builds after it, carry, so no reader expects it here:

- **C2b, plans and blocks** (`c2-scope.md` §3): the plan vocabulary in the task object (`Task.plans`, `canonical` learning sets so a block hashes, `_vocabulary`/`DEFAULTS` widened); each condition naming its structure, and so its counting table (C2a counts every trial by the default structure's, or its block's); a plan chosen at run start (`RunSpec.plan`, `wlx run --plan`, `StartRun`/`CheckRun`, the page's picker, and `Service._start`'s one field, its welfare summary's one item); the block-sequence policies -- fixed, cyclic, seeded and **balanced, within each run** (the PI's decision 7) -- and by criterion with advance and fall-back; block and run time limits, in the loop and never in `Session._ends`; the per-advance progress check, which ends a plan that cannot draw with a sentence (XC-364); load-time checks of plans, each to the warnings-over-refusals rule; plan tables in the review report; a plan's blocks declaring drumming (`Block.drum`, built here, which is also how drumming is on from a run's first trial) and their own counting (`Block.counting`, built here); and the "until C2" comments (`task.py`'s `Task` docstring and `trial`, `simulate.py`, `review.py`, `taskd.py`'s "The structure this run plays", `preflight.py`).
- **C3, varying conditions and the live-edit layer**: the designed orders (priming, sequence-balanced) and **their repeat rule, default none** (§16.4; XC-361); drawn values and **whether a repeat's are fresh or the same**, a drum's repeat keeping its own (§16.5; XC-362); the change log, which **takes over the clear's and the drumming switch's records, with their reasons** (XC-363).
- **The warnings build's**: the checks of stop, pause and the welfare limits about once a second inside a long trial (§6.8, §20 item 4; XC-373), and durations to frames at load (§6.2).
- **A control that un-counts a trial already counted**, if the PI reads his "clear a specific trial in the count" that way ("What the PI should know", item 6).
- **wl-preproc reading the new fields** (XC-365, the ask written).
- **Real tasks' counting tables**, set with the PI in wl-xtasks: the reference tasks' are engine test material, run only in the simulator (his question of 2026-10-13).
- **Calling the staircase at each trial boundary** (XC-242): `next_params` steps up on a miss from Task 10, and nothing calls it yet.

- **A scheduled "stop after N trials" counting trials rather than presentations** (Appendix B, item 20): it counts every presentation, as built, and the PI kept it so on 2026-10-18 ("Keep counting presentations"); the page's option says so.

- **Each task leaves the suite green**, as run (the header). Also: Task 2 moves the one test fixture that would no longer load (`ONE_STATE_TASK`) in the commit that adds the refusal; Task 3 migrates every test that named a removed set, and the flat-run hang test to a run of two, in the commit that removes them; Task 9 gives both full test frames the three new fields in the commit that adds them; Task 10 fixes every reference task the new warning would otherwise flag in the commit that adds it.
- **The hot path**: nothing new in a frame. `run_trial` computes its cap once, before its first frame; the table is read, a repeat placed, a warning fed and the rows written between trials.
- **External facts**, each with its source and date: what wl-preproc reads of `runs.jsonl` and `trials.jsonl` (`wl_preproc/events/rigruns.py` and `rigtrials.py` through the worktree's link, read 2026-10-11); the placement defect's numbers (the scope's probe, reproduced on Task 3's code, 2026-10-12); the free slots' lateness (a seeded probe on Task 4's code, 2026-10-13). No paper is newly cited; each key the plan names is in the library, for a claim decisions.md already records it for.
- **Placeholders**: `YYYY-MM-DD` is the day a step runs, as the house plans use it. No other: the PI's answers to Appendix B are dated, 2026-10-13.
- **The amendments of 2026-10-18.** Decision 17 -- Task 12 (TSK-26); decision 18 -- Task 13 (TSK-26, TSK-52); the controller's rulings: I1 -- Tasks 15 and 9 (TSK-28); I3 -- Task 7's fix round; M1 -- Task 14 and Task 11 (TSK-31); M2 -- Task 13's words, Task 11's ask; M3 -- Task 14; M4 -- Task 9; M5, M6 -- Task 15, Task 11's ask; M7 -- Task 13; M8 -- Task 12; M9, M10 -- Task 11 (XC-380, XC-381); the smaller ones -- Tasks 9 and 14. Task 8's fix round is unchanged; its counts are one higher than written (the header's note).

Task 7's review: Important 1 -- decision 7; Important 2 and minors 4 to 6 -- its fix round; minor 3 -- moot. **Names used before they are defined**: none -- `first_answers` (Task 12) is switched by `train` (Task 14); `ConditionReport` and `Tally.drummed` (Task 15) are read by Task 9; `Training` (Task 14) by Task 9's page. **Each task leaves the suite green**, as run (the header's note). **The hot path**: nothing new in a frame; every count, switch and report is read or written between trials.

## Appendix A: Welfare summary items

**No function on architecture.md's welfare-critical list changes**, and the list is not widened. Task 11 compares each byte for byte at the branch's base and tip: `bounds.py`, `welfare.py`, `marks.py` and `stranded.py` whole; `preflight.gate` and `out_of_cage`; `Service._open`, `_end`, `_unended`, `_close_stranded`, `_start`, `_unasked`, `_folder_name`; `taskd.Session._ends`, `_hold`, `_manual_reward`, `set`, `_schedule` and `OUTSIDE_A_RUN`; `link._setting`; `cli._settle_departure`, `_settle_return` and `main`'s `_marks.depart(session, departure)` line; and `Session._command` with C2a's three branches taken out (two since the plan's approval, the third since its revision of 2026-10-18).

For the PI's numbered summary before C2a merges, three welfare-adjacent behaviors, and since 2026-10-18 a fourth, **for his approval**:

1. **Two console commands pass through `Session._command`** (Task 8), part of which is welfare-listed -- its `held` pass-through, which hands every manual reward to `_manual_reward`, and its one `except` line, which makes a malformed setting a refusal. **Neither listed part changes**: the clear (one trial or the rest) and the drumming switch are two `isinstance` branches beside the pause, each handing its command to a method outside the list (`Session._clear`, `_drum`), and Task 11 compares the rest of the function byte for byte. Neither touches a reward, a bounded setting, a clock or the pump. Because `_hold` drains commands through `_command` too, a clear or a switch sent while paused is applied while paused, as a scheduled stop is.
2. **Repeats and drumming can make a block longer** (Tasks 4 and 6): a condition with no limit is repeated until done, and a drummed trial comes back until it is right. Each repeat is an ordinary trial: a correct one pays the same `reward_correct`, through the same path; the out-of-cage limit (`welfare.must_stop`, read between trials and while paused) ends the session whatever a block still owes, and nothing in C2a reads or changes it. A trainer can move past a trial with *clear one*, past a condition with *clear the rest*, or switch drumming off, and **after ten failed tries in a row of one drummed trial the console says so** (the PI's answer of 2026-10-12), so a drum left on is seen. *Since 2026-10-18 (the PI's decisions 6 and 10): clear is a training aid, so the trainer switches it on first, with one click; and a block that ends on a criterion runs until its criterion is met -- past its targets it draws every condition again -- where it faulted when its targets ran out, so until build C2b's time limits the out-of-cage limit, a scheduled stop or a person ends one whose criterion is never met. No live run has such a block before C2b.* In the two detection tasks a broken target hold is now a target break, shown again where their own table counts a block, where it was a wrong answer that paid (the PI's answer of 2026-10-13); a plain "run N trials" run, every live run until C2b, still pays every trial.
3. **A trial now ends at five minutes at the session's rate** (Task 5), where it ran up to 100,000 frames -- about 417 s at 240 Hz and 833 s at 120 Hz; **below 333 Hz**, where 100,000 frames take 300 s, which covers the lab's 120 and 240 Hz panels, that is shorter than before, and the console now says when a trial reaches it. **The warnings build's `longer-than-declared` warning now names five minutes** (`check.TRIAL_CAP`, from `run.LONGEST_TRIAL_S`): its approved wording, "about 417 s today, 5 minutes once C2a is built", is what C2a makes true. The welfare limits are still checked between trials only, so the longest stretch without a check is shorter than before at those rates; the checks about once a second inside a long trial (engine spec §20, item 4) are the warnings build's XC-373. **The holds' new bounds** (Task 10) make no trial longer than it could be before: each is no longer than the setting that bounded its state.

4. **A third console command passes through `Session._command`** (Task 14; new on 2026-10-18, **for your approval**): the training switch -- *clear* on or off, and how a percentage counts drummed answers -- is a third `isinstance` branch beside the pause and the two you approved for Task 8, handing its command to a method outside the list (`Session._train`). **Neither listed part changes** (the `held` pass-through that hands every manual reward to `_manual_reward`, and the one `except` line), and Task 11 compares the rest of the function byte for byte with all three branches taken out. The switch touches no reward, bounded setting, clock or pump; like the other two, sent while paused it is applied while paused. ***Approved by the PI on 2026-10-18 ("Approve").*** *The amendments of that day (his decisions 17 and 18, the controller's rulings on the revision's review) change no welfare-listed function: the scheduled stop's option text changes on the page only, `Session._schedule` and `_ends` untouched, and Task 11's comparison reads every line `same`, the three branches `added once`.*

## Appendix B: For the PI to confirm

**Answered on 2026-10-13, item by item** (your decision 4: each task declares). Each item below is one thing a task's counting table says, with your answer: items 1 and 2, "Approve"; item 3, "Task names it, required"; items 5 to 13, "Accept, target break". Items 4, 7 and 11 also carry your decisions of 2026-10-11 and answers of 2026-10-12. Four words are used throughout: **counted** -- an answer, which pays one of the trials its condition is owed; **used up** -- paid as a presentation, but marked an abort, not an answer (the spec's "spent"); **shown again** -- pays nothing, and the condition comes back within the next few trials, never the very next (the spec's "owed"); **pays nothing** -- neither paid nor shown again, the block's order bringing the condition back as it would anyway (only "run until N correct" uses it, for a wrong answer).

**Four reading rules, for every task**

1. **Your answer, "Approve"**: four answers to "what does this trial do to its condition's count": counted, used up, shown again, or pays nothing. A task's table gives one of them to every way a trial can end, and the review report shows the whole table for each task.
2. **Your answer, "Approve"**: every way a trial can end, and its class in every task unless the task's own line changes it (none of the five reference tasks has one):
   - **Counted** (your decisions of 2026-10-07): correct; an early or late response to the target; a wrong target; an early or late error; a correct rejection; a false alarm.
   - **Shown again** (2026-10-07): a fixation break; no fixation; a lost tracker; a fault.
   - **Used up or shown again, as each task says** (your decision 4, no default -- items 5 to 10): a target break, a catch break, a motion break and a long blink, once the stimulus has appeared; and **looking somewhere that is neither target nor distractor** (`abort`), which is not a break but ends a trial after the stimulus as one does.
   - **No response**: counted, a miss, where the task says withholding is an answer (both detection tasks, your decision 6); otherwise as a break after the stimulus.
   - **The five-minute cap**: item 4.
   - **Under drumming**: everything that is not right -- a correct answer or a correct rejection -- comes straight back and pays nothing, whatever the table says (your decision 2).
3. **Your answer, "Task names it, required"** -- "Each task's counting table names the step where its stimulus appears, and the load check refuses a table without one. A cued task names the cue step. A small extra declaration in every task; the five test tasks name their target or array step." A break before that step -- a long blink during the fixation hold -- is shown again in every task, as a fixation break is, since nothing was presented; a break after it follows items 5 to 10. A table naming no step, or one its structure does not have, is refused at load, saying which structure and why. The five reference tasks name `stim_on` (both detection tasks), `array_on` (both search tasks) and `await_fix` (calibration, whose target is up from the trial's first frame).
4. **The five-minute cap -- your answer** (decision 3): a trial reaching it is recorded as a hang, as today, and counted as a missed acquisition -- shown again, like no fixation; in a plain "run N trials" run, one of the N; and the console says so.

**Each reference task** -- **your answer to items 5 to 13, "Accept, target break"**: "Take the plan's tables, make leaving the target early a target break in the two detection tasks, and give visual_search_training no repeat limit." The question said these tables are engine test material, run only in the simulator, and that real tasks' tables will be set with you in wl-xtasks.

5. **`fixation_detection`**: no response is a miss, **counted as an answer** (your decision 6 -- "a miss (counts, used up)", read as: it counts, and it is never shown again; it is not marked an abort); a break after the target appears is **shown again**. Since item 6, that includes leaving the target before its hold is complete, which is what a trainer shaping the hold wants; on the rig a blink arrives as a lost tracker, shown again in every task.
6. **Both detection tasks: leaving the target before its hold is complete is a target break -- your answer**, as `visual_search` already called it, where it was a wrong answer: the landing is the report and the hold a criterion, and these tasks show no distractor. Shown again by items 5 and 7, strobed as a target break, and, being no answer, it does not move `adaptive_detection`'s staircase (the science review's recommendation).
7. **`adaptive_detection`**: no response is a miss, counted as an answer (your decision 6), and **a miss steps the staircase's contrast up -- your answer** of 2026-10-12 ("A miss counts as an incorrect answer, so after misses the target gets more visible"); a break after the target appears, leaving it before its hold among them (item 6), is **shown again**. Reason: a break leaves no answer, and a one-up two-down staircase classes each trial as a positive or a negative answer, so a break is neither [@levitt1971transformed] (p. 472); shown again, it is presented at the staircase's contrast then, not the one it broke on. With no target-absent trials, the point the staircase converges on (70.7%) is a hit rate, which "could depend on a change in only criterion, only sensitivity, or both" [@luo2015neuronal] (Introduction): fine for training, worth knowing for recording.
8. **`visual_search`**: a break after the array appears -- leaving the target before its hold is complete, no saccade before the deadline, a long blink -- is **used up**: counted as presented, marked an abort, never shown again. Reason: shown again within a few trials, the array would repeat its target's color and position, and macaque pop-out performance depends on whether the previous trial's features and target position repeat [@bichot2002priming] (abstract) -- priming of pop-out, the lab's own measure [@westerberg2020priming] (full text); used up, the trial sequence stays as drawn. Caveat: an animal could skip a condition by not responding and it would never be flagged, since the console shows no count of trials used up per condition.
9. **`visual_search_training`**: a break after the array appears is **shown again**. Reason: a training variant, so each condition is practised as planned; a delayed repeat is a standard training option [@nimhmonkeylogic2026task] ("repeat delayed").
10. **`calibration`**: a break after the target appears is **shown again**. Reason: its target is up from the trial's first frame, and every target must be fixated to be fitted; in practice calibration ends only as a fixation break or no fixation, both shown again under item 2, so this line changes nothing today.
11. **No repeat limit in `fixation_detection`, `adaptive_detection`, `visual_search` or `calibration`** -- your decision 5, and for calibration **your answer** of 2026-10-12 ("No limit": "As drafted. The trainer moves past a target with the console's 'clear' button."): each condition is repeated until done, and *clear one* or *clear the rest* moves past by hand.
12. **No repeat limit in `visual_search_training` -- your answer**, as built: a color or set size the animal refuses returns until done, and a trainer moves past it with *clear one* or *clear the rest*.
13. **No reference task declares drumming**: a trainer switches it on from the console, for a block or a run.

**Added 2026-10-18: the revision's engineering calls**, each a reading the code had to settle where your decisions did not say, with what it costs if wrong. **Answered 2026-10-18**: items 14 to 19, 21 and 22, "Approve all"; item 20's scheduled stop, "Keep counting presentations". Items 15, 16 and 20 were amended that day, after his answers 17 and 18 and the controller's rulings, each saying how.

14. **A streak is a rule of proportion 1.0** -- "N correct in a row" is every one of the last N answers right; any lower proportion is a percentage. Your "first answers only" setting changes how a percentage counts drummed answers and leaves a streak judging every try (Task 12). Cost if wrong: a block declares which it is, one field.
15. **A block can no longer name which outcomes its rule judges** (`Block.criterion_over`, removed): every answer is, and only answers, as you decided; a narrower set, or a non-answer, could only contradict that. Nothing but a test set it; build C2b's plans would have. Cost if wrong: the field back, limited to answers. *Amended 2026-10-18 (your decision 17, "Yes, where the block says so"): the one exception is a block whose own table counts a break as paying -- a hold-training block -- whose break is judged, a miss: a block's own table can count a broken hold, or a failure to acquire fixation (decision 19), as counted, and then it is judged a miss; and a block's table cannot make no response an answer where the task does not (the controller's ruling M8).*
16. **Past its targets a criterion block draws every condition again, by its own order**, a condition moved past by its limit or a *clear* included, until the criterion is met. Nothing is owed then, so a break is not shown again and no limit is reached, but drumming goes on; each row reads as any other, and the end row's counts show the trials past each target (Task 13). Cost if wrong: leave moved-past conditions out, one condition in the draw. *Amended 2026-10-18: a break's row there still says `owed`, which past the targets means "no answer, not shown again" (the controller's ruling M2: not reclassed), and the block ends only once a drum waiting is answered right (your decision 18, "Finish the drum first").*
17. **A criterion never met no longer faults the run; it runs until the out-of-cage limit, a scheduled stop or a person stops it**, as a block a person never clears does today. Build C2b's block and run time limits would end one sooner; no live run has a criterion block before C2b (Appendix A, item 2's note).
18. **Each training switch holds for the run it is made in**: every run starts with *clear* off and a percentage counting every answer, as it starts with drumming off, until per-animal profiles (XC-379). Cost if wrong: hold them for the session, one place.
19. **"Trials" counts every drawn trial but a drum's repeat**; an ordinary repeat -- a break shown again a few trials later -- is a trial of its own, as it always was (your decision 5 was about drumming). Cost if wrong: count it with the trial it repeats, one line.
20. **These still count presentations**: each line's index and trial number (your decision 5: "Every presentation keeps its own line and trial number"), the run's end row's `trials`, telemetry's trial count, and **a scheduled "stop after N trials"** -- whose code is on the welfare list (`Session._ends`, `_schedule`), so it is left as built; it stops sooner under drumming, never later. Cost if wrong: that stop counting trials, a welfare-list change for your approval. *Amended 2026-10-18 (your answer, "Keep counting presentations", and the controller's ruling I1, keeping your decision 5): the trial count the page and /health show, and the end row's `trials`, now count trials, with the presentations beside them (the end row's `presentations`); what still counts presentations is each line's index and trial number, the scheduled stop -- its option says "after N more presentations, drummed tries included", page text only -- and the strip's rate, now "presentations/min".*
21. **"Right first time" is a trial whose first try was right**: one whose first try was a break, then right, is not; its first answer still is what "first answers only" judges (your answer "Judge its first answer"). Cost if wrong: count it right first time, one condition.
22. **A trial used up -- a break the task spends -- ends the trial's count too**, as an answer does ("1: an answer ends the trial"), since it is not shown again either. Cost if wrong: the count runs on through it, one condition.
